# Meta-language vision and architecture

This is the authoritative specification of what meta-language is for and how it
is built. Everything else in this repository serves it: where the root
[README](../README.md), a package README, the
[grammar subsystem documents](grammar/README.md), the
[parity roadmap](parity-roadmap.md), a case study or a code comment disagrees
with this document, this document is the contract and the other text is a
defect to fix.

This document describes the **target**. It does not claim that the target has
been reached. What is actually implemented and verified today is the generated
[requirement ledger](issue-195-requirement-ledger.md), computed from observed
test execution. The [current state](#current-state) section lists the largest
known gaps.

## Sources of truth

The scope below comes from
[issue #195](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-language/issues/195) and
every requirement-bearing comment on
[pull request #196](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-language/pull/196).
After that pull request merged, [issue #199](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-language/issues/199)
carries its remaining scope and adds release and memory-regression obligations.
Its requirement-bearing comments and the audits on
[pull request #201](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-language/pull/201)
remain binding. Direct conversation requirements are captured verbatim under
`parity/sources/` and registered with their content hashes; their timestamp
records the capture, because the conversation does not expose a source revision
time. They do not create another architecture specification.
Four files keep it honest:

| File | Role |
| --- | --- |
| [`parity/issue-195-sources.lino`](../parity/issue-195-sources.lino) | Source register. Lists the issue and every requirement-bearing comment with its revision time, byte count and sha256 content hash, plus the solver status reports that carry no requirements. `npm run check:issue-195:sources` checks the register against the ledger. `npm run check:issue-195:sources -- --online` also compares it with the live discussion, and fails when a registered source was edited or a new comment has not been classified. |
| [`parity/issue-195-requirements.lino`](../parity/issue-195-requirements.lino) | Atomic requirement ledger, generated by `js/scripts/issue-195-requirements.mjs`. Each row names its source, the scope (language, version, edition, construct, aliases, extensions), the expected behavior, the runtimes that must implement it, the implementation entry points (`null` where none exists yet), the verification cells with their fixtures and assertions, and a `traceability` record. That record links the row to its section here, its feature inventory, every registered source that covers it, the CI workflow that evaluates it, the packages that ship it and the downstream evidence it needs. A protected baseline preserves rows, fixture coverage, runtimes, aliases and each row's source, expected behavior and scope; corrections to wrong assertions follow the evidence policy below. |
| [`docs/issue-195-requirement-ledger.md`](issue-195-requirement-ledger.md) | The generated pass/fail report of the ledger for one exact commit. |
| This document | The architecture and scope that the ledger rows implement. |

A later source supersedes earlier architectural guidance where the two conflict,
but no source removes a language, semantic, testing, cleanup or delivery
requirement. The
[September 28 audit](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-language/pull/196#issuecomment-5870004788)
is kept as historical evidence. Its failure counts are not the current status.

Historical tooling inputs may retain an independently checked Git object
certificate as Links data. The supported SHA-1 profile keeps exact UTF-8 commit
text, every complete ordered tree on the original path, modes, object identities
and original blob bytes. Verification recomputes each object identity against
the independently supplied original revision and path, with explicit resource
bounds. Normal verification needs neither the Git database nor a network read;
automatic capture reads the original objects. Unsupported encodings and formats
refuse. This establishes immutable source membership, not semantic equivalence
between concepts. The original loan receipt uses this path without changing its
source revision, expected contents or predecessor assertions. The object framing
follows the [Git object model](https://git-scm.com/book/en/v2/Git-Internals-Git-Objects).

## One common language of links

Meta-language provides **one common, extensible language, represented
recursively as links and serialized in Links Notation**. It covers:

- language grammars and the concepts they define;
- complete concrete syntax trees, produced by default;
- optional abstract syntax trees that keep explicit provenance back to the
  concrete syntax;
- semantic representations: bindings, types, universes, effects, ownership,
  macros and notation, and proofs;
- the transformations and translations over all of the above.

A grammar, a parsed document, a semantic annotation and a transformation rule are
all links in one network. None of them lives in a side table, an opaque string
payload or a host-language callback that the network cannot inspect.

The goal is coverage of **all existing languages**. The popular languages,
dialects, grammar formats and downstream workloads named here and in the ledger
are mandatory minimum inventories, not a permanent shortlist.

Both runtime packages, the Rust crate `meta-language` and the npm package
`meta-language`, implement the same features with the same observable behavior.
[`parity/language-features.lino`](../parity/language-features.lino) enforces
this, and the runtime-parity rows of the ledger check it again on shared
fixtures.

### Global concept space

Exact graph identity includes every positive unsigned 64-bit identifier. The
JavaScript `UnsignedLinkId`/`UnsignedLinkNetwork` profile keeps this domain
explicit, alongside the original safe-Number profile. Identity arithmetic and
decimal/byte conversions use inspectable shared limb definitions, automatically
translated to Rust. The exhausted allocator is distinct from the largest link.
`MLU64N01` snapshots preserve original identifiers, repeated and cyclic edges,
metadata, exact term registrations, selected syntax and allocator history. A
strictly ordered exact-ID directory reversibly changes coordinates for the
existing complete `MLDSNP02` metadata/cache codec; it is not a new upstream
binary standard. Dense views let existing graph algorithms process these
coordinates while their complete inverse directory stays explicit. This proves
neither concept meaning nor semantic equivalence. New dense nodes require
explicit original-ID allocation; direct exact-ID integration throughout every
parser, schema and CLI remains an obligation. The source draft and deferred
fixtures do not establish runtime parity or completion.

All grammars of all languages inhabit **one global concept space**. It includes
programming languages, grammar-description languages, document formats and
natural languages, together with the translation rules over their concepts.
An unshared concept belongs to this same space; it retains its own identity and
qualified language provenance until an equivalence proof permits reuse.

The regular-grammar refinement draft retains each preceding complete model,
decision table, grammar and concept source graph in a flat history directory.
The complete original learner and the actual refinement-validation definition
appear once as shared typed Links. The journal preserves added positive and
negative observations in order, including duplicates and statistical multiplicity.
Source-generated decisions check exact corpus prefixes, selected settings and
distinct source identities. Reload revalidates every recorded learner decision
and grammar against its actual supported algorithm, then checks continuity.
Changed definitions, lost observations, unreferenced history and reused source
identities refuse. Historical groups retain their original local identities;
deterministic reruns establish no semantic equivalence certificate. Old and
refined sources can inhabit one dynamic session, whose default remains CST.
This version relearns deterministically from the retained corpus under the
existing RPNI/EDSM/ALERGIA-style profile. It establishes neither modern discovery,
unknown-language completeness, asymptotically incremental performance nor semantic
equivalence. Eight journal steps and finite graph/learner budgets bound actual
reconstruction; exhaustion refuses instead of dropping history. These checkpoints
and their deferred text/binary/native controls remain unexecuted code drafts.

The automated translation and import pipeline first copies every concept and
its recursive links into this space without losing rules, aliases, occurrence
order or provenance. Deduplication then constructs a checked one-to-one
correspondence between the complete reachable definitions. The correspondence
must preserve recursive structure, operational context, semantic distinctions,
binding, effects and any proof assumptions. The certificate records the paired
concepts and the evidence used to validate every pair. Equal names, samples or
accepted text alone do not establish equivalence. An unsupported or ambiguous
proof leaves both identities intact and preserves the candidate correspondence
as data.

Every verified equivalent concept resolves to the same stable global identity
at every occurrence in every grammar and translation rule. Its original
language-specific spelling, concrete syntax and source location remain aliases
and provenance on those occurrences. Distinct integer, whitespace, binding,
effect and universe meanings retain distinct identities even when their names
coincide. The complete space and its certificates serialize as links, reload
without the original grammar sources and yield identical decisions in both
runtime packages. Shared identity simplifies translation by exposing a reusable
meaning; target-specific syntax and faithful encodings remain explicit rules.

An implementation revision that admits a new input domain does not establish
equivalence with the preceding operation. Current declarations retain qualified
source identities until their complete meanings can be compared. Historical
definitions remain available through authenticated Links source archives,
including their full declaration and import context, original source hash and
revision. Reconstructing old source bytes proves preservation of that historical
definition; it does not prove that the expanded current operation has the same
meaning. Naming inventories can reference the exact archived definition without
reassigning its identity to a changed current body.

Historical source guards authenticate the complete current module before
inverting recorded, disjoint byte intervals and checking every predecessor byte.
Original source hashes, receipt edges, assertions and refusal cases stay exact;
current translation behavior uses current imports and independent controls.
Naming extraction retains every actual current source name. Scoped historical
inventory views may additionally recover names from complete independently
certified predecessors, with explicit revision and archive provenance. Current
modules and the additive driver must match their complete reviewed source pins;
unknown successors refuse. Every unchanged naming policy, coverage assertion
and positive or negative fixture still applies to the combined inventory.
The original two views remain separate from additive profiles. Each additional
row carries its own certified source revision and archive provenance; its
unchanged inventory extractor must derive the original name and identity.
Complete current source hashes bind the selected checkout, including copied
roots. A source certificate preserves a historical definition without assigning
its meaning to a changed current declaration.
Native evidence includes an experimental Rust source only when its exact path
belongs to the actual registered native test module. A JavaScript driver or an
unregistered Rust filename cannot supply that evidence.

The current global grammar parser reuses its complete matching-context comparison
once within each provenance invocation. Every production still checks its own
complete definition and every original mismatch remains observable. The default
budgets are unchanged. Certified predecessor and current source views retain
all frozen diagnostic inputs, both producer identities, actual current type and
import owners, and the complete diagnostic calls. A frozen CI overlay continues
to use its separately authenticated producer rather than a new current source.

Canonical native, JavaScript tooling and test sources, captured requirements
and the version-qualified npm manifest retain LF checkout bytes on every supported platform. Exact native-module registration
checks therefore inspect the same bytes on Windows as on other runners. The
check-only synchronous source hooks select Node.js `>=22.15.0 <23.0.0` or
`>=24.0.0 <25.0.0`; uncovered versions refuse. The lower bound follows the
[Node.js synchronous hook API](https://nodejs.org/download/release/v22.15.0/docs/api/module.html#moduleregisterhooksoptions).
The source models retain their complete preceding version profiles and Git
membership as Links data. Local controls exercise the active Node 24 runtime;
Node 22 execution and native Windows behavior still require CI.

Historical generators verify the complete inputs of their recorded revision.
An exact current successor may supply that historical view only after its whole
digest and certified byte inverse pass; current artifact gates remain separate.
Unknown edits receive no historical authentication. A historical generator
refuses to overwrite a newer current artifact. Native packaging, primitive
column loans and source-only callback changes therefore retain the original
receipts and every assertion without replacing the current implementation.

Private inverse-model and numeric-observation fixtures retain their explicitly
recorded complete source inventory. The current grammar-context source profile
is fully validated before projecting its complete authenticated predecessor for
those historical controls. Current wire publication keeps the full current
profile. Every original parser case, graph limit, native constructor, numeric
validator and expected output remains exact; a source fingerprint refresh never
replaces those expectations. Current overlay providers include the strict
optional Boolean string-representation flag and its preservation during whole
semantic rechecking. Their complete current and preceding Git objects and exact
module inverses extend the existing provider chain without changing earlier
revisions or historical overlays.

Incremental import validates that historical identities and origin aliases are
unique before reusing them. It reserves every historical identity before
allocating new current classes. A restored origin whose old identity is still
owned by a surviving, distinct concept receives a deterministic unused identity;
an identifier collision cannot discharge an equivalence obligation. Surviving
identities remain stable, and sharing still requires the current recursive
proof after source-free Links reload. Shared JavaScript decisions generate the
native identity selection and history admission; host sets supply cardinalities.
The verifier and identity operations are emitted as separate closed modules,
preserving every source declaration and the verifier's existing correction
contexts. Their identical array runtime is shared after whole-module comparison.

The archive-backed parser factory, `createGlobalConceptSpaceParser`, validates
the complete linked archive, including its proofs and final redirects, before
compiling an exact archived source identifier. It reconstructs every grammar
and resolves recursive imports and embeddings solely from that archive. The
factory owns the source selection, concept space and resolver; factory and
per-parse options cannot replace them. Default output retains the qualified
concrete tree and embedded provenance. Existing any/all selection, cycle
diagnostics and shared parser budgets remain in force. The `records` option
configures archive validation only.
Explicit custom records do not materialize the shipped default record values;
the default values are parsed only when no custom records are supplied.
Discovery serialization retains the producer's explicit container aliases
between primary and first owned definitions. Equal independent containers
remain distinct. The native adapter preserves the default encoder, key order
and signed zero; native execution still requires CI verification.

Archives retain the original grammar format and raw declaration presence.
The effective matching context includes the format's default: implicit PEG
repetition remains possessive after reload, while an explicit generalized
override retains its behavior. Shared decisions determine this default in both
runtimes. Format spellings alone do not prevent proven equivalent concepts from
sharing; correspondence checks their actual operational context. Historical
archives retain the format facts they actually contain.

Discovery reload rechecks every current certificate exactly. A wholly historical
certificate may omit source-format facts only when its implicit matching default
still agrees with the current source grammar, or matching was explicitly
declared. Revalidation projects only those absent provenance fields for this
comparison, checks all other definitions and proofs, and returns the full
current space. Mixed provenance and changed present facts refuse; implicit PEG
cannot use the historical generalized-default profile. Original discovery
archives remain byte-exact compatibility fixtures.

### Recursive formal concept definitions

Every concept has a deep recursive formal definition made of inspectable links.
Definitions can use other concepts, including shared and mutually recursive
references; identities, bindings, axioms, selected primitives and foundational
assumptions remain explicit through serialization and reload. An English
description or a declared meaning signature complements that formal definition
and cannot substitute for it. Definition comparison checks obligations under
the declared model and preserves an unknown result when a proof is unavailable.
It does not promise to decide arbitrary program equivalence.

The current typed-program adapter serializes checked semantic records, ordered
containers, scalar values and explicit binding, declaration, datatype and
constructor references as links. Constructor identities belong to their actual
datatype; equal constructor names in different datatypes remain distinct.
Pattern bindings have inspectable name and type records, and each case retains
its own lexical scope, including shadowed names and ignored ordered slots.
Reload validates structural and semantic traversal limits, types, ownership,
constructor arity and match coverage before emission. The data, constructor and
match projections reuse the existing surface checker and compare the complete
rechecked semantic program. Shared decision data supplies reference roles,
operand traversal, projection instructions and role-specific absent fields.
Type records also distinguish serializer-owned neutral values from unknown
fields. The actual native type declarations define five omitted false flags:
array, record, map and set references, and record openness. A source-checked
Links table supplies their decisions to JavaScript and automatically translated
Rust. Explicit false and absence have the same type identity; true remains
distinct. Other fields, values and record roles retain their existing validation.
The schema extension authenticates each complete predecessor and keeps its
original author, receipt, model and property order independently checkable.
The optional program field `utf16Strings` retains absence, explicit false and
explicit true through both archives and complete semantic rechecking. A present
value must be Boolean. Shared checked JavaScript decisions select the existing
UTF-16 emitter when the program, explicit emitter profile or actual operations
require it; native host fields and their strict Boolean reader are generated
from recorded source adaptations. Encoding metadata does not replace checked
types, bindings or string operations. Native execution remains a CI obligation.
Owned function calls project through the actual declaration's rooted path,
including root functions, while rechecking its name, qualified identity and
module path. Caller-local bindings cannot capture a callee's default initializer
through a shortened projected name.
These source-free semantic graphs emit canonical target syntax; byte-identical
unchanged-source preservation remains the separate lossless CST contract.
Integral metadata uses the value of its linked Number. The fixed type bit-width
role accepts equivalent finite spellings such as `32`, `32.0` and `3.2e1`,
including the same type inside an owned proof. Its supported widths still come
from the shared typed schema. Native decoding normalizes only that declared
role before reading its integer field; primitive floating values and all other
scalar roles keep their existing interpretations.
Partial callback contexts retain the supplied parameter types and keep an absent
return type absent while the body checks. A checked function always has a concrete
return type, including callbacks returned through conditional, binding, effect
and match expressions. Matching branches derive their concrete result from an
actual checked branch before coercion; contextual parameters cannot substitute
for the callback's declared arity or actual result.
Array callback admission and Rust rendering share the complete checked
JavaScript rule module, including its generated continuation. Hosts retain actual
receiver, element and accumulator type owners, ordered argument checks and
the outer expected context for a reduce initial value. Complete source-free
program fixtures retain both explicit reference profiles, original refusals and
independent values and execution traces. The current reference profile refuses
callbacks that declare the receiver parameter; separately admitted cases do not establish
support for that parameter or shared receiver mutation. Native execution remains
a CI obligation.
Direct native programs can retain dynamic proof widths as floating Number
values. Their selected validation uses a bounded private comparison view with
the same fixed-width record adapter as Links decoding; the original proof
values, own-field order, nulls and unknown fields remain observable and checked.
Native surface adapters preserve optional-field presence independently of the
field's value. An omitted array spread flag remains omitted, while explicit
false remains present. Binding annotations distinguish omission, explicit null
and a concrete type through the native host representation. These adapters keep
the existing source-free checker and wire observations; a default value cannot
replace a presence distinction. Assertion propositions use the same shared
operand and binding plans during encoding, reload and complete rechecking,
including each quantified binding's actual lexical identity.
Optional JSDoc parameter markers retain their declared value type and explicit
undefined absence through surface, checked and linked representations.
Documentation defaults do not supply runtime initializers. Provided arguments
evaluate once in source order before applicable lazy defaults, which retain
their callee scope and initializer order through source-free rechecking.
Known record, tuple, Map and Set bindings have JavaScript `typeof` tag `object`.
The evaluated-query extension captures calls, field reads, constructed values
and other represented operands before yielding their statically known tag.
Unit results produce `undefined` only after evaluating their actual operand.
Pure bound/literal queries keep their earlier constant representation. Shared
source rules select these cases and automatically generate native definitions;
unknown value kinds still refuse. The two former refusals (`typeof fail()` and
`typeof [1]`) retain their exact source inputs and gain translation plus actual
abrupt/normal-output assertions in both runtime controls. This correction follows
[ECMAScript 2025 13.5.3.1](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/ecmascript-language-expressions.html#sec-typeof-operator-runtime-semantics-evaluation),
which evaluates the operand and gets its value before returning a tag. Deferred
controls retain heap effects, getter calls, abrupt Error requests, void effects,
complete typed codecs and four target emissions. No control has run locally.
Unresolvable names, temporal dead zones, symbols, proxies and HTMLDDA remain
separate source/environment obligations, rather than inferred from a type tag.
An optional query requires a known nonempty present-value tag and retains its
null and undefined alternatives without discarding operand effects or
exceptions. A raw JavaScript mixed array in an actual `Option<Tuple>` context
checks against the ordered tuple elements before optional wrapping. This
context supplies neither an immutable-product proof nor a new union
interpretation. Native execution of the expanded contract remains a CI
obligation.
Inferred element annotations are written back for both empty and nonempty
arrays. Native `semanticRet` metadata retains the source's semantic return
alongside its original annotation and checked result. Nested JSDoc Map and Set
arguments reuse the existing recursive type-separator rules. Unsupported union
target encodings produce typed diagnostics with their existing message and
failure ordering; the historical diagnostic receipt remains separate.
Map and Set constructors retain one evaluated iterable expression and their
complete checked type through editable semantic links. Shared constructor
decisions preserve the actual contextual type owners, primitive key admission,
diagnostics and all four target templates, including insertion order, Map value
replacement, NaN equality and positive-zero key normalization. The two projection
plans append to the existing schema without changing its model, prior plans or
historical source receipts. Complete JavaScript source and text and binary
program replays drive automatic native generation; native behavior remains a CI
verification obligation.
Owned occurrence paths likewise compare the numeric value of an actual Number
position, checking the actual array bounds through the shared tuple-index rule.
Equivalent integral spellings retain their stored scalar, including negative
zero naming position zero. String fields remain distinct from numeric array
positions, and fractional, out-of-range and unsafe positions are refused.
Rust source admission retains parsed declaration and lexical pointer origins
before projecting immutable references and standard boxes into the value core.
It checks the actual owner and consumed pointer layer under that selected model;
complete trait, borrow, lifetime and destructor semantics remain separate
obligations.
The opt-in `Rust source function signature 1` model records actual supported
header type owners, ordered parameter and result positions, immutable reference
and box layers, and lifetime positions and binders as an owned function facet.
Both runtimes validate its arity, types, ownership and exact shape after
source-free Links reload. Nineteen independently specified headers exercise the
capture; seven source refusals and ten malformed graph edits check its boundary.
Body value edits remain executable while the source facet stays intact. Existing
archives omit this facet by default. The independent
body ownership obligation also requires actual constructor, clone, dereference
and receiver owners, together with the borrow and lifetime relations that connect
them to those headers. Header capture alone does not prove a body's String
conversion, a move from a borrowed non-Copy value or an escaping reference.
These operation facts and their checked links remain required independently of
the source signature facet. The independent
`primitive borrowed Rust function header 1` emission model retains direct
primitive `f64` and Boolean result headers and direct or multiply borrowed
immutable primitive parameters. Ordered ASCII lifetime binders and reference
layers come from the checked owned facet after source-free Links reload.
Scoped value-copy bindings connect those headers to the checked value body.
Twelve independently specified headers and actual lifetime, wrapper and body
edits exercise the boundary. The selected program contains flat leaf functions;
callers, imports, effects, boxes, mutable or borrowed results and custom type
owners require further interface proofs. Malformed generated flags,
unrepresented parameter fields and contradictory context flags refuse before
emission. Retained headers and value copies establish no general pointer,
resource or ABI equivalence. Effect threading
refuses a captured interface rewrite until its signature transformation is
proved.
The independently selected `closed primitive borrowed Rust call interface 1`
model retains a flat primitive leaf and an actual caller whose whole body is one
checked call to that leaf. Each pure primitive argument evaluates once in source
order into a hygienic scalar binding before the leaf's exact immutable reference
layers are constructed. Both owned source signatures survive Links reload;
actual argument, lifetime, reference-layer and body edits exercise the boundary.
Twelve complete admitted programs and twelve exact source refusals cover this
draft. Reimport of actual emitted definitions checks the standard semantic
model; this does not establish that the emitted binding-bodied caller satisfies
the original direct-call selection. Borrowed results, static lifetime demands,
recursive or chained calls, imports, effects and general generated runtime
modules still require their own interface proofs. Both selected header models
reject unknown function, parameter and expression fields before emission.
Scoped lifetime-only function headers now retain ordered input binders and
immutable reference spelling. Reference-bearing results with named binders still
require an instantiation and outlives proof before admission. Type and constant
generics, lifetime bounds and mutable references remain separate obligations.
Unshadowed standard vectors and immutable slices now have bounded source
admission for direct scalar and string elements, with read-only empty checks and
length conversion explicitly projected to the Number model. Ownership moves,
borrowed results, mutable arrays and full generated runtime wrappers still need
their separate obligations.
Proven immutable borrowed `str` receivers can select closed ASCII predicates
through `starts_with` or direct `chars().all`. Complete shared JavaScript
decisions generate the native admission rules. Callbacks contain only admitted
ASCII tests and character equalities; captures, effects and unknown owners
refuse. The receiver evaluates once. First-character queries remain bounded;
the all-character value interpretation uses the existing eager point collection
and therefore needs successful allocation and O(n) temporary storage. It does
not claim the lazy source iterator's allocation behavior. Actual JavaScript
array-literal spreads reuse the existing checked source-iterator operation,
including Unicode code-point iteration and its ownership refusals. Internal
concatenation and mathematical array construction retain their original paths.
Complete emitted Rust is checked separately from definitions that omit runtime
context. Default CST reconstruction remains independent of executable source
interpretation. The unrestricted JavaScript regular-expression helper still
needs its observable input coercion and caller-domain proofs.
Rust decimal integer separators before the existing supported suffixes use a
shared generated lexical boundary decision, preserving exact integer text and
UTF-16 source spans. Its original boundary decision retains unsuffixed
trailing-separator and non-ASCII suffix-boundary refusals. A separate source
composition also admits the exact proved Rust whitespace boundaries by reusing
that decision with its existing ASCII space observation. Unknown Unicode suffix
continuations and non-Rust whitespace remain refused; JavaScript numbers,
floating boundaries and radix gaps retain their existing interpretations.
JavaScript line comments and the supported return, break, continue and throw
boundaries use one shared generated ECMAScript line-terminator policy. Carriage
returns, line separators and paragraph separators cannot hide active statements
or turn an automatic empty return into a value return. A line terminator inside
a block comment retains its restricted-production meaning; other languages keep
their own comment boundaries.
Rust string continuations and CRLF normalization
use shared checked rules while preserving original source-token spans.
Cooked Rust strings admit the exact simple escape prefixes and two-digit
seven-bit hexadecimal escapes; invalid prefixes, non-ASCII hexadecimal values
and isolated source carriage returns refuse. A Rust-specific Unicode decoder
counts one through six actual hexadecimal digits and retains permitted interior
and trailing underscores without a fixed escape-width cap. It reuses the same
hexadecimal digit operation and one lazy full-source unit buffer. Its result is
the existing scalar, malformed or unsupported escape meaning; the generic
decoder and other source languages retain their original contracts. Rust token
separation uses its exact eleven Pattern White Space code points and permits a
byte order mark only at original source offset zero. All of these decisions
retain original UTF-16 spans and serialize their policy as shared links.
Rust raw string literals retain their complete zero-through-255-hash delimiters
and literal backslashes. Their shared reader reuses the existing source scalar
and line-break decisions, normalizes carriage-return/line-feed pairs and refuses
isolated carriage returns and unpaired source surrogates. Decoded values enter
the existing String paths with their original source intervals. Closed models
that select fixed decoded runtime strings retain those values and actual
operation owners; the explicitly raw-excluding generated namespace model keeps
its refusal.
Cooked and raw Rust byte strings have a separate lexical representation. Shared
source rules retain all eight-bit escapes, ASCII content, literal raw delimiters,
CRLF normalization, continuations and original source spans. Identifier suffixes
remain part of their actual lexical token; they do not acquire typed expression
meaning. The byte payload has a canonical hexadecimal transport rather than a
host text encoding. Unicode byte escapes, non-ASCII source, isolated carriage
returns and malformed delimiters refuse. The complete dependency closure is
editable typed Links and generates native decisions automatically. Earlier raw
text decoder controls retain every input and their `notRaw` expectations; the
full lexer now recognizes actual raw byte strings under the explicit byte-string
profile. Complete earlier assertion sources and the Rust Reference basis for this
correction remain available. Static byte-array references, their lifetime and
ownership semantics still need typed translation support.
The bounded Unicode scalar source text byte model exposes actual UTF-16 units, exact
UTF-8 length, individual bytes and scalar-boundary slices in both packages.
Shared numeric rules generate the native byte decisions; thin host adapters
validate primitive text and numeric offsets before materializing the result.
Unpaired JavaScript surrogates are refused rather than replaced. A complete
pinned formatter probe still refuses its unsupported formatting, parsing and
ownership operations; these byte primitives alone do not establish formatter
translation.

Standard immutable text emptiness and zero-argument `String::new()` use actual
unshadowed standard owners, including proved imported aliases. Inclusive
primitive `f64` range membership consumes the complete
`(start..=end).contains(&value)` query and projects it into scoped bindings and
comparisons. Bounds and the borrowed argument evaluate once in source order;
NaN, infinities and signed zero retain the primitive comparison behavior.
Five shared source cases and eighteen exact refusals cover both runtimes.
General range values, iterators, custom comparison owners and unsupported
ownership layers require separate interpretations.

Direct standard fixed integers and Booleans now have an ordinary source draft
for min/max methods and qualified std::cmp::min/max calls. A declared
or aliased std namespace keeps its separate source interpretation. Function
values retain their callable obligation. Shared decisions require the actual
owner, width, sign, proven direct receiver and independently matching operand.
Unsuffixed integer syntax receives the actual receiver's type context; full
checking still rejects invalid literals. Pointer-sized and fixed-width owners
remain distinct even when their representations coincide. Every receiver and
argument evaluates once in source order before existing conditional operations
select the result. Integer minimum keeps the first equal value; maximum keeps
the second. Boolean selection uses existing Boolean conditions in false-before-
true order. These choices follow [Ord](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/cmp/trait.Ord.html).

Complete shared definitions generate Rust source and typed Links. Native hosts
transport actual source facts and build the existing AST forms, without a
second primitive selection algorithm. Sixty-nine original programs retain
all fixed widths and boundary values, Boolean truth tables, contextual literals,
calls, recursive selection, independently specified native function types,
complete output traces and both generated products. Every shared premise and
actual source refusal remains a deferred control, together with both Links
transports and all target emissions. This establishes no runtime evidence.
Borrowed Ord results, generic/custom trait resolution, floating-point NaN/zero
semantics, clamp/panic behavior and unsupported literal receiver origins remain

Explicit standard min/max imports now reuse that same selection definition.
Ordinary use declarations record only an actually admitted standard comparison
owner in the existing module-local alias map. Calls resolve the actual alias
before shared role admission; local source bindings prevent interception.
Grouped, renamed, raw-keyword and Unicode aliases retain their source identities.
An alias in one module cannot change an independently declared function in
another. Six complete original programs, independent native types and exact

Direct standard signed integers now have source drafts for is_negative,
is_positive and signum. Shared postfix plans express the complete result using
existing typed constants, comparisons and conditional nodes. Every plan operand
refers to one captured receiver; even the signed minimum requires no absolute
value or overflowing negation. Predicates return a standard Boolean; signum
retains the original signed width and pointer-sized source owner. Both source
adapters project the same generated plan into the existing AST algebra. These
rules follow the standard [signed integer methods](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i128.html#method.signum).

Ninety-five complete source programs retain all signed widths and exact

Ordinary direct fixed integers now have source drafts for saturating_add and
saturating_sub; signed integers additionally support saturating_neg and
saturating_abs. Their shared postfix plans place conditional guards before

for unary negation/absolute value. Every actual operand evaluates once in source
order before any plan occurrence reads its captured value. Actual widths,
signedness and usize/u64 or isize/i64 owners remain separate premises. Exact
decimal bound magnitudes reuse the existing power-of-two definitions and shared
digit operations; full integer bounds never pass through binary64. These rules
follow the [signed](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i128.html#method.saturating_add)
and [unsigned](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.u128.html#method.saturating_add)
standard operations.

Saturating multiplication has an independent shared source plan over the same
exact integer domain. Both operands are captured before any threshold decision.
Zero guards dominate division; signed thresholds divide MIN only by a positive

truncation toward zero before deciding whether the product is representable.
The actual multiply appears only in a safe conditional branch. Source adapters
reuse the generic AST projector with existing division/multiplication nodes;
there is no native replacement algorithm. The preceding four-operation
saturation definition and its unknown-multiplication controls stay unchanged.
This follows the [standard multiplication operation](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.i128.html#method.saturating_mul).

Standard checked integer arithmetic has a shared source draft for checked_add,

Standard checked_shl/checked_shr now reuse the complete existing source shift

Signed unsigned_abs and signed/unsigned abs_diff now have shared guarded source
plans with their complete unsigned result domains. Negative magnitude adds one
before signed negation, casts the resulting nonnegative value, then adds one in

orders the original source values and computes the selected unsigned modular
difference through representable guarded arithmetic. The complete MIN-to-MAX
distance therefore fits the matching unsigned width without a larger host

matching uN owner. Original operands are captured once, and exact associated
aliases/immutable Copy receivers retain their source signatures.

The six standard exact integer-to-binary64 From domains now have a complete

Integer associated constants now have a source draft for MIN, MAX and BITS

Immutable reference chains to actual standard Copy integer primitives now have

missing, inconsistent, malformed or unrepresented positions refuse. Recursive
nominal fields reference the actual owned datatype links instead of unfolding
their definitions infinitely. Source-free validation, checked projection and
text/binary reload preserve this facet. Equal portable field layouts and equal
function headers therefore cannot erase distinct field meanings such as
`usize`/`u64`, `isize`/`i64` or boxed/direct nominal positions from complete formal
definitions. Plain parsing retains its independent omission behavior. This
records admitted field types; it establishes neither Rust aggregate ABI/layout
reconstruction, generic or named-lifetime datatype contexts, arbitrary traits,
ownership/coercions, callable-field target encodings nor semantic equivalence.
All future original-source, codec, target and meaning controls remain deferred
until the complete requirements draft.

The explicit `retained scalar Rust function interface 1` emission model
reconstructs canonical native scalar endpoint types from these actual
version-two owners. It retains Boolean, binary64, Unit, fixed integer and
pointer-sized primitive meanings, plus immutable-reference input layers and
their represented lifetime binders. Closed owned functions may call each other
across modules and recursively inside scalar expressions. Every actual call
target and ordered argument type is checked before changing any interface.
Borrowed inputs are copied into core scalar values; ordered hygienic temporaries
convert arguments into the target's source types before recreating references.
Returned source values convert back into core values at the call boundary.
Only the pointer-sized scalar representation needs an extra numeric conversion;
the emitted library refuses other pointer widths with a compile-time guard.
The actual checked scalar graph has at most 100000 queued expression references,
including repeated edges; distinct nodes are checked once.
Automatically translated shared decisions and syntax supply both runtimes;
native adapters project owned IR instead of duplicating the algorithm.
Existing portable, leaf-header and borrowed-call profiles retain independent
contracts. Non-scalar headers, borrowed results, static copied call borrows,
imports, generated functions, runtime effects, source-loop facets and additional
qualifiers require their own compositions. This mode establishes neither stable
foreign ABI layout nor complete public visibility, source syntax identity,
primitive source typing or full own-codebase translation. Complete original
sources and typed function assignments are future independent native controls;
no execution evidence follows from the source draft or its retained history.

The independent `retained mathematical Rust function interface 1` model reuses
those complete scalar headers, conversions and closed-call obligations while
admitting checked primitive Rust bit operations and binary64 absolute value,
floor, ceiling, truncation and predicates. Every mathematical operand remains
inside the same scalar graph and resource budget. Actual operand typing and
integer/Boolean/IEEE interpretations stay with the existing core checker and
emitters; shared automatically translated rules select the admitted operations,
arity and result kinds. Ordered argument materialization, immutable borrowed
inputs, pointer-sized boundaries, module and recursive calls retain their full
earlier checks. The earlier interface still refuses mathematical expressions.
Other mathematics, approximate transcendental parity, random effects, aggregate
or callable interfaces, checked-shift optional results, source loops, borrowed
outputs and additional qualifiers remain separate obligations. Complete original
programs, independent native function types, both generated native products,
both codecs and four-target controls are authored, unexecuted definitions.

The independent `retained textual Rust function interface 1` model adds actual
standard owned `String` endpoints and immutable `String`/`str` inputs to the
same checked scalar call graph. Every original reference layer and lifetime
remains in its header. Borrowed string referents are copied into the owned core
Unicode value through the actual standard `ToOwned` operation. Calls materialize
each complete argument once, then recreate `str` borrows through standard
`String::as_str`, or recreate the represented `String` reference layers. Owned
string results, exact UTF-8 byte lengths, string equality, scalar expressions
and admitted mathematical operations compose under their existing meanings.
The source-free boundary checks byte-count language, representation, complete
arity and unsigned 64-bit result type in both packages. Earlier source interfaces
retain their string refusals. General string transformations and ordering,
borrowed outputs, pointer/capacity/allocation observations, arbitrary ownership,
mutation, custom string traits and aggregate interfaces remain separate required
work. This value interpretation does not assert allocation identity, complete
native ABI fidelity or full own-source translation. Original/native products,
independent function types and codec/target controls remain deferred drafts.

The separately selected `retained string predicate Rust function interface 1`
adds complete Unicode scalar string prefix, suffix and substring tests to that
textual interface. Its shared JavaScript definition admits only a Boolean
result, two actual String operands and the existing starts/ends/includes
operations. Both complete operands remain in the checked scalar graph, with
the original expression fields, reference headers, source owners, lifetime
binders, call ordering, static-borrow restriction and resource budgets. The
existing common operations supply all four target emissions; the native
adapter receives the same automatically generated admission rules. Earlier
interface models retain their predicate refusals. These meanings follow the
standard [string predicates](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.str.html#method.contains).
Independent original/generated/native values, exact function types, Unicode
and empty-pattern cases, text/binary reload, source-free edits and reimport of
the actual emitted textual predicates are deferred controls. Unknown raw
fields must reach the Links decoder before a native host DTO can discard them.
No character or closure patterns, normalization, locale behavior, arbitrary
transformations, UTF-16 offsets, borrowed outputs, general ABI or ownership
completion follows from this draft.

The additional `retained constructed Rust string function interface 1` admits
complete scalar String concatenation within that same checked interface graph.
Its shared rules require the operation's fixed implicit String domain, or an
explicit matching String domain, a String result and two complete String
operands. Other binary operations keep their required domain records. The
existing formatter supplies string-valued fields, literal braces,
named and repeated positional fields, with each actual explicit argument
captured once before field assembly. Existing calls, predicate operations,
source owners, lifetimes, byte measures and graph budgets retain their checks.
Both adapters reuse the automatically translated decisions and the existing
four-target concatenation operation. Native product controls compare actual
returned strings directly, avoiding any dependence on Rust Debug escaping.
Both codecs and independent values/types are required after reparsing the
actual emitted formatting definitions. Earlier models retain concatenation
refusals. General Display conversions, formatting traits/specifiers, arbitrary
macros, borrowed results, string ordering, allocation identity and full
ownership or source-language completeness remain required work. All these
controls are unexecuted source drafts.

The independent `retained displayed Rust string function interface 1` adds
ordinary fixed-width integer and Boolean Display to the same complete string
graph. The actual operand remains a checked fixed-width value or Boolean, the
result is String, and console representations refuse. Every operand is visited
under the existing graph budget. Source width, signedness, primitive owner,
reference layers, lifetime binders, ordered captures and checked calls remain
explicit. The existing primitive conversion and concatenation definitions supply
all four targets; no handwritten native display algorithm is introduced.
These meanings follow the standard [Display contract](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/fmt/trait.Display.html)
and [formatting syntax](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/fmt/index.html).
Earlier interface models retain the identical original Boolean refusal input.
Independent minimum/maximum/zero values, complete original/generated products,
native function types, both codecs and actual emitted-definition reparse are
deferred controls. Floating-point and unbounded-integer formatting, custom
traits, radix/precision/locale specifiers, borrowed outputs, general ownership
and full own-codebase support remain requirements.

Actual standard String clone/ToOwned results and one borrowed str ToOwned
result retain a standard owned String source owner, no borrowed lifetime and
no Copy evidence. Shared source rules select only the real standard owner and
supported zero/one-reference cases before adapters update those facts. This
corrects stale str ownership in emitted-literal reimport without changing the
existing value operation or claiming a complete reference-method resolver.
Multiple-reference results, custom owners and the remaining borrow/move domain
retain their separate obligations.

Ordinary Rust source addition has an additional standard String value draft.
An actual owned standard String on the left and one immutable standard str or
String reference on the right project to the existing complete concatenation
operation. Shared JavaScript decisions check actual source owners, kinds,
models, reference layers and proven immutable facts; those decisions generate
the native rules and Links definition. The right String remains borrowed, and
each intermediate result keeps its actual standard owned String origin.
Declaration collection stays provisional until actual bodies are re-read.
Selected const, local-state, type-namespace and scalar-loop profiles keep their
separate interpretations. This value projection follows
[String addition](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/string/struct.String.html#impl-Add%3C%26str%3E-for-String);
it does not prove complete source move/loan validity, allocator identity,
custom trait dispatch or additional reference coercions. Original and both
generated products, source-free reload, four emissions and retained constructed
interfaces have unexecuted controls. Full own-codebase support remains required.

Ordinary source parsing also recognizes scope-checked qualified standard
`Clone::clone` and `ToOwned::to_owned` calls. Relative and leading-`::` standard
paths select shared source rules only in an unambiguous standard namespace.
Actual primitive Copy, String/str and direct scalar vector/slice facts check one
immutable receiver reference before lowering to the existing value or complete
copy projection. Real standard vector-copy occurrences retain their fresh owned
result/argument evidence. The declaration collection pass keeps a provisional
call only until every actual body can be re-read against complete source facts.
User-owned `std` namespaces retain ordinary resolution. No general trait/UFCS
dispatch, standard function values, general borrowed-result
flow, deep String/nested vector ownership, all namespace contexts or full emitted
runtime parsing follows from this draft. A portable single-copy definition has
deferred emit/reparse controls under the existing core value meaning basis;
that comparison is not a certificate of complete Rust source ABI equivalence.

The additional `immediate standard Rust string views 1` source interpretation
admits the actual qualified `String::as_str` call only as a complete argument of
a checked named function. Its input must have nonstatic immutable `str`
references, and its result must be an actual owned standard String or a known
primitive scalar. The original standard String receiver has exactly one
immutable reference. Each argument owns a bounded context, restored even when
parsing fails; parentheses and extra reference layers retain the complete
original view span and the exact expected source depth. A derived expression
cannot inherit permission merely because it contains a view. Shared JavaScript
decisions generate the native source and editable Links definition. These
constraints preserve the limited immediate borrow meaning described by
[String::as_str](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/string/struct.String.html#method.as_str)
and [temporary scopes](https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/destructors.html#temporary-scopes).
Complete retained textual definitions have deferred emitted-source reparse,
independent value and native function-type controls in both packages. They
retain actual emitted ToOwned/as_str source rather than restoring an original
body. Additional value copies do not prove graph isomorphism. Stored/escaping
views, static consumers, borrowed/aggregate/callable results, unknown/dynamic
consumers, general loans, moves and ownership remain required implementation
work. No behavioral control, native compilation or parity execution has run
for this draft.

The ordinary
source call interpretation additionally admits fresh scalar `vec!`
arguments. Actual macro source intervals supply constructor evidence, separate
from the explicit result profile and local binding evidence. Scoped body tails,

original-Rust, codec, four-emitter and decision controls remain unexecuted drafts.
General vector ownership and remaining source call forms remain required.
The independently selected
`closed guarded function returns 1` profile admits direct function returns and

The source profile, independent vector-result selection and user provenance

the existing default-profile return diagnostics remain exact.
Under this explicit completion profile, the emitter's exact two-literal

including braces, escapes, empty text and Unicode. Dynamic formatting, other

for one unconditional loop over direct `f64` and Boolean values. Its owned

The independently selected `standard primitive Rust binary64 owner 1` source

Alias, namespace, occurrence and body mutations recheck the complete ownership

Custom owners, `f32`, broader constant evaluation and general imports remain
separate obligations.
Operation fixtures compare non-NaN results by exact bits, including signed zero,

and explicit array-length conversion to unshadowed `f64` under the Number length

receivers and other numeric methods retain their separate obligations.
Read-only scalar `mut` parameters require a complete assignment-free body and
ordinary source-owner validation. Mutable writes, generated runtime wrappers

The independently selected `owned JavaScript scoped bindings 1` emission model
renames checked local binding records and their actual uses through the existing

reservations without reserving unrelated global declaration names in that

does not exempt reserved prefixes or permit counterfeit loop ownership.
Selected emission validates every owned field and its value shape before
native decoding can discard unknown fields. The entire checked program is
reconstructed and compared against its semantic projection, including ordinary
function bodies, recursion metadata and effects. Unknown binding metadata,
malformed known values and stale semantic facts refuse after serialization and
reload. A revalidated declaration-owned scalar loop projects its actual
continuation root and path in both runtimes. Empty-option source ownership can
compose with scoped emission only after its independent proof validates.
The default repeated-source refusal remains exact. The native adapter also
retains the exact `Option(Float, undefined)` type and `None(undefined)` value
observed in genuine JavaScript output, including lexical and temporal binding
ownership of `undefined`. Optional absence is not coerced to Unit. Other option
elements, nonempty option values and null remain outside this bridge. Native
compilation and executable parity for the combined draft await CI; broader
runtime-module coverage and a complete intermediate-type fixed point remain
separate obligations.

JavaScript declaration admission records actual parser scopes, original identifier
sites and initialization points before lowering or builtin normalization. A shared
generated rule resolves the nearest binding. Blocks and loop heads own scopes;
inline declaration wrappers share their existing owner. Deferred closures require
an invocation-order proof for reads before an outer binding initializes. Named
functions and parameter defaults explicitly refuse unrepresented mutable module
captures in either source order. Parameters and constant shadows retain their
existing checks. These parser-local records add no fields to checked programs or
saved Links. Native hooks transport the same facts to the generated rule; native
compilation and executable parity remain CI checks.

Ordinary arrow/function-expression callbacks also admit flat required object
parameters through an explicit closure context. They reuse the complete original
required-pattern rules, actual bound names and body entry bindings. Ordinary
method closures retain the existing receiver-independent restriction. Generated
logical arguments are existing semantic parameter records with actual pattern
origins; scope facts do not fabricate source identifier tokens or spans. Alias
bindings, positional arguments, outer shadows, captured values and local writes
retain the normal callable/cell behavior. Strict directives still check the
actual body. Named declarations keep their separate profile; defaults, nested,
rest and computed patterns, async/generator closures and complete receiver
semantics remain requirements. Shared context/pattern definitions and their
dependencies serialize as editable typed Links and generate native decisions.
The new deferred source/product controls establish no execution evidence.

The independently selected `closed empty primitive Rust option 1` source model
admits flat zero-argument standard `Option<f64>` functions whose complete body
is an explicitly typed empty constructor, together with an empty main. The
owned `sourceEmptyOption` facet records actual source, resolved and canonical
result and constructor paths, type arguments, namespace premises and body
sites. Eight independently specified owners, thirty-two exact source refusals,
twenty-nine malformed owned edits and eight raw null or unknown edits bound
this draft. Actual emitted Rust definitions replay with a canonical empty main;
the resulting complete JavaScript output reimports and executes. A coherent
declaration rename exercises source-free editing of this singleton-result
boundary without inventing a changing empty value. The old generic-type
refusal remains exact. This separate facet does not broaden the existing Rust
source-signature model. Nonempty options, other elements, null, imports,
attributes, full runtime wrappers and other source-model compositions still
require independent proofs. Native compilation and executable parity run in CI.
The independently selected `resolved Rust type namespace 1` source model
resolves actual header spellings against the type namespace before projecting
primitive value types. An enum and a value function with the same spelling
retain distinct owned identities. Source-free links preserve each parameter
and result spelling alongside the existing source signature; validation resolves
the ordered identifier segments again against the actual program catalog.
Both direct emitters recheck these namespace facts in each runtime before
rendering, as linked validation and rechecking do. JavaScript container guards
require plain or null-prototype program roots and inspect own and inherited
source-marker descriptors without invoking accessors. Surviving source facts
on callbacks or inherited function records cannot bypass the selected
interpretation; exhausted inspection limits refuse before a fallback.
Crate, self and parent anchors retain their declared scopes, and value calls
retain their actual function targets. Checked spelling edits and value-body
edits remain executable. Fifteen independently specified sources, twenty-four
exact source refusals and twenty-five malformed graph edits bound this draft;
the preceding fourteen sources, seventeen refusals and twenty-four malformed
graphs remain unchanged.
An actual attached standard `Clone` derivation on a field-free datatype is an
owned `sourceUnitCloneDerivation` facet. Its datatype identity, constructor

Both runtimes recheck that premise before admitting a direct owned datatype
clone. Two derivation sources, six exact method refusals and three malformed
derivation graphs exercise this boundary. Other method implementations and
associated constructors require separate proofs.
Actual emitted definitions reimport with the emitter's declared name mappings
and a canonical empty main. Independently validated primitive namespace facts
compose with both borrowed-header models and scoped JavaScript emission.
Imported aliases, library positions, generic types, borrowed results and
constructor-overwritten identities require separate proofs. The existing
source-signature model's exact refusals remain required. This draft does not
establish arbitrary runtime-wrapper replay or pointer, outlives and ABI

The independently selected `closed pure Rust unit calls 1` model retains flat,
zero-argument private Unit helpers and their actual ordered calls from main.
`closed public pure Rust unit calls 1` also retains each actual bare public
keyword and its owned source position. Header and body forms, call paths and

Twelve private sources, nine public sources and sixty exact source refusals
bound these interpretations. Five complete JavaScript programs replay their

the actual target bindings. The complete runtime shell, standard-library calls,

The JavaScript-first `closed pure Rust unit runtime shell 1` draft is a
separate whole-artifact interpretation for actual emitted closed Unit programs.
It consumes the entire lexer-owned crate, retains its lint attribute, public
helpers, private worker, standard argument scan, thread builder, stack request,

as inspectable linked operations with original spans and actual binding links.
The checked main retains an independent worker-context marker, so removing only
the root proof cannot erase its source runtime operations and assumptions.
Its `successful closed pure Unit worker observation 1` explicitly assumes
successful argument enumeration, thread creation on a word size of at least 29
bits, normal worker completion and join, and no asynchronous termination.
Only ordered owned Unit calls, empty output and normal completion are projected
under those assumptions; failure-path equivalence, allocation, timing and physical

saved operation graph and checks its Unit projection through the real checker.
Coherent linked declaration, lexical-binding and bounded resource edits must pass
both checks, and Rust emission renders the actual owned shell rather than replacing
it with a default wrapper. No source envelope or selected-function substitute is
used. Comments and layout are not a byte-preserving CST round trip under this
semantic model. The old default and both Unit models retain their exact refusals.
The native adapter uses checked owned positions internally and validates real
declaration, token and operation link identities before projecting saved graphs;

executable cross-runtime replay checks remain CI obligations before this draft
constitutes runtime parity support.
The isolated JavaScript-first `closed generated JavaScript Unit module 1`
experiment reads the complete actual emitted strict module, retaining every

binding checks and primitive Number source intervals remain checked through

cycles and actual JavaScript execution; complete emitted Rust is re-read under
its four operation-bound successful-runtime assumptions. Incoming assumptions
remain explicit witnesses. Default and generic public APIs do not enforce this

the bounded experiment and both freshness checks.
Named source spans compare safe, nonnegative integral offsets by value, including
equivalent decimal and exponent spellings. Source-position negative zero remains
invalid while primitive floating-point negative zero retains its value.
JavaScript callable declarations retain their actual first token through the end
of the last consumed token, in UTF-16 code units. A following declaration's

never collapses it to the opening keyword. Normal, expression and scalar-loop

The [finite receipt register](../parity/self-translation/complete-declaration-receipts.lino)

The reference-callback emitter revision adds a separately pinned descriptor

The neutral-type rule revision adds a third finite descriptor edge for the

The whole preceding reader and register remain recoverable, and the loan receipt

The next descriptor edge retains the actual current inference and emitter

It changes only thirteen provenance fields, preserves every emitted definition

Historical controls select that exact predecessor; current causal controls

The explicit string-order schema successor reuses its existing complete model,

Two independently selected owned-text source models retain local ownership

The separate JavaScript-first `closed generated owned Rust module 1` experiment
reads five complete actual emitted owned String/i32 modules, including their

Source-free callee, declaration-name and Unicode literal edits revalidate those

initializer scope transport and source-defined punctuation/BoundName decisions.
Named functions, constants, namespace methods and nested closures retain their
separate context admission. Original field access and original initializer
expressions then enter the existing undefined-only default checker and inference
constraints. The source wrapper disappears into ordinary typed IR before both
codecs and four emissions; no forged index or new Core node is introduced.
Present represented fields, including undefined/null payload distinctions and
local binding writes, use their existing field/type protocol. An additional
shared field-domain decision admits undefined for a missing normal name on an
ordinary closed record. Both inference solvers retain actual owner/field terms
until that decision resolves; open missing shapes keep waiting. Both checkers
capture the actual receiver before a missing field selects its lazy initializer.
An absent own field with a standard or compatibility Object prototype spelling
retains an explicit inherited-property obligation. Its actual own-field shadow
continues through the normal field protocol. This follows
[Object prototype properties](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/fundamental-objects.html#sec-properties-of-the-object-prototype-object)
and [compatibility properties](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers.html#sec-additional-properties-of-the-object.prototype-object).
Arbitrary prototype/getter/proxy domains, whole-parameter defaults and the remaining
initialization environments remain obligations. Thirty-four future values,
nine refusals and the exact former closure default-refusal input have deferred
original/emitted/native controls and full predecessor assertion sources. Nothing
in this draft establishes execution evidence or complete language support.

A guarded dynamic record-index draft uses actual positive `Object.hasOwn`
conditions over local closed-record/String functional binders. An actual unary
negation places the same fact in the other branch, including early-return
continuations. Logical and/or supply a fact only to the actual right-operand
branch selected by their short-circuit condition. Binding either operand again forgets the fact. Source cells,
aliased/different keys and extra/effectful guard operands supply no stronger
premise. The source-defined exact UTF16 key search selects canonical named
projections; the adapters retain receiver-before-key evaluation and existing
optional/nullish payload semantics. The checker facts do not become trusted
source facets or replace runtime conditions. The resulting ordinary typed IR
retains the guard and selected source operation through both codecs and four
emissions. Unchecked dynamic reads retain their inherited-property obligation;
heterogeneous value unions, mutable key cells, broader control-flow/alias proofs
and exotic property protocols remain requirements.

The immediate actual `Object.keys(localRecord)` producer can also supply this
membership fact to its inline callback's actual element binder. The complete
existing array callback role definitions determine that binder, including the
second `reduce` parameter; `Array.from` mappers use their existing mapper role.
Both checkers identify the original callback node, install the fact only after
its parameters bind, and reject owner shadowing or exposure of the live key
array. A receiver parameter could replace later keys and therefore supplies no
membership guarantee. Saved key arrays, key aliases and indirect callbacks need
their own origin/alias interpretation. This context is private checker state;
it disappears into the same ordinary guarded lookup IR. Complete source rules,
including the callback role dependency, generate the native decisions. Thirty-six
future source values, twelve exact refusals and scope/node/role invalidation
controls are drafted without execution.
The own-membership distinction follows [Object.hasOwn](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/fundamental-objects.html#sec-object.hasown).

The own-entry read draft extends the same actual argument capture and own-field
order used by keys/values. Closed ordered records and represented indexed or
primitive receivers select their original keys and homogeneous payloads only
after every argument has evaluated. An ordinary source function pairs exact
ordered keys with opaque wrapped payloads, explicitly checks their lengths and
retains nullish payloads independently of absent slots. The complete original
source generates native code and its editable definition. A specialization
changes only the declared opaque payload type; ordinary checking validates
both the original ordering entry and the new pair entry and constructors.
Adapters project those declared entries into the existing positional tuple
value domain before emission. Both runtime checkers and linked archive
validation check exact key/value/result types, and both preparation passes
lower pairing through the same complete source. This adds fixed-position entry
reads and local parameter writes, not pair mutation or reference comparison;
existing tuple obligations remain. Heterogeneous dynamic values and the full
own-property descriptor/getter/proxy/Symbol protocol remain separate requirements.
Future controls cover insertion/numeric order, spreads, extra-argument effects,
nullish coercions/payloads, UTF16 String properties and exact errors, along with
codecs, four emissions and original/native products. None has executed. See
[Object.entries](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/fundamental-objects.html#sec-object.entries)
and [EnumerableOwnProperties](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/abstract-operations.html#sec-enumerableownproperties).

The runtime JavaScript frontend also collects actual parsed declarations and
BoundNames, with original source spans, independently of predeclaration
lookahead. Its shared early-error definition rejects same-frame lexical
collisions, lexical/function collisions and lexical declarations that redeclare
parameters in the immediate callable body. Nested blocks, sibling blocks and
loop-head/body scopes remain separate. Native parsing projects the same actual
scope and parameter facts into the source-generated decision. It runs before
the existing temporal-read validation and type inference. It does not replace
existing directive, mutable-pattern or temporal guards. Script/Module-dependent
ordinary-function duplicates, var/import/class bindings, Annex B and scalar-loop
shortcut admission retain their separate obligations. Future paired controls
cover accepted shadowing, exact syntax refusals, decoded escaped names, complete
definition codecs and four emissions; none has executed. The shared rule follows
[block early errors](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations.html#sec-block-static-semantics-early-errors)
and [function early errors](https://tc39.es/ecma262/2025/multipage/ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes.html#sec-function-definitions-static-semantics-early-errors).

The opt-in `closed binary floating point function` model gives closed Rust and
JavaScript functions a declared common abstract value interpretation. It guards
flat function declarations with binary64 and Boolean parameters and results,
finite decimal literals, lexical variables, local bindings, conditionals and
closed recursive calls. Rust admission requires an owned
`Rust source function signature 1` facet whose parameter and result owners are
direct primitive `f64` or `bool`, with no wrappers or lifetime bindings.
The selected function's synthetic call wrapper must use the actual ordered
parameter bindings and a distinct owned target. Shared admission rules are
automatically translated into Rust and accept only declared arithmetic,
comparisons and Boolean negation. The linked primitive basis states IEEE
arithmetic, rounding to nearest with ties to even, truncated quotient remainder,
all binary64 result values and unobservable NaN payloads. Recursive equations
denote partial functions. The ordinary rounding environment is an assumption;
host stack exhaustion and machine resource limits lie outside this abstract
value interpretation.
Finite literal text remains unchanged in typed archives and formal definitions.
Only the temporary comparison graph replaces admitted literal meanings with
validated IEEE binary64 bit patterns, preserving shared references and distinct
signed zeros while equating decimal spellings that round to the same value.
The shared spelling rule excludes a leading plus, leading or trailing decimal
points and padded leading zeros. Source language and the owned source signature
remain inspectable in the actual typed graph and origin provenance. Comparison
omits their meaning relations only after every profile guard holds and the
linked primitive basis matches its declared model. Immutable body references
and boxes may pass only through the existing checked value-preserving erasure;
pointer identity, allocation, deallocation and their resource failures are
outside this model. The original source-qualified model keeps its
source-language premise. Eight actual
Rust/JavaScript function pairs exercise recursive certificates and source-free
reload, including direct and mutual recursion. A checked pair grounds reusable
global identity; changing an operator preserves distinct identities. Unsupported
types, effects, defaults, source interpretations and counterfeit premises retain
their refusals. Qualified source-header emission and reimport, cross-ABI
behavior, complete effects and resource equivalence, and formal definitions for
every catalog concept require separate evidence.
The separate opt-in `closed boolean function value` model observes the selected
callable rather than its public namespace. It admits only primitive Boolean
parameters, results and intermediate values, Boolean literals, negation,
conjunction, disjunction, equality, local bindings, conditionals and closed
recursive calls. Conjunction and disjunction preserve short-circuit evaluation,
including a recursive operand that is not reached. Its meaning root is the
actual owned target of the checked ordered entry wrapper. Declaration spellings
remain in the original archive as origin aliases; parameter order, actual
binding and recursive target identities, operators and the complete primitive
premise remain meaningful. Every Rust function retains its owned direct-Boolean
source signature. Numbers, strings, references, defaults, guards, effects and
captured interpretations refuse. The JavaScript interpretation assumes callers
supply the declared Boolean arguments; this is no untyped whole-module or public
namespace equivalence. The prior models and their original refusals remain.
Shared JavaScript admission decisions generate Rust automatically. Prepared
definitions include the actual named-import erasure declarations, complete
module concrete syntax and an explicit empty-main Rust checking harness.
Source-free text/binary proof controls and independently authored truth values
run in JavaScript; native execution and matching certificates require CI.
A separate `closed boolean function declaration value` basis selects an actual
owned function through a `selected function declaration` link on the formal
root. It retains the original checked program, declaration identities, source
signatures and const proofs. A source proof must never be copied onto a renamed
