generated wrapper. The original strict Rust const interpretation validates the
original program before the new value basis can omit its source qualification
from mathematical comparison. The actual archive and native emission retain
that qualification. The old wrapper basis, source-qualified meanings, fixtures
and refusal boundaries remain independently checked. This direct entry does
not admit arbitrary const calls, compile-time evaluation or broader input types.
Formal relation comparison also validates the actual label self-points and
anonymous relation edges under their declared interpretation. Matching spelling
cannot hide a different namespace, definition or identity premise.
Linked text conversion rejects unrepresented array operands and undeclared
Rust floating-point display before admitting a formal definition. Existing
record definitions remain available; their admission does not verify complete
source record or console formatting.
The experimental record text rules distinguish ordinary ECMAScript coercion
from Node console inspection. Their bounded ordinary record trees and complete
checked algorithm serialize as inspectable links and reload without the source
objects or source code. Thirty fixtures exercise both models in JavaScript,
including emitted JavaScript after reload; native generated Rust verification
runs in CI. These rules cover safe integers, booleans, bounded big integers and
ASCII text in shallow single-line records. Complete source admission and emitter
integration, floating-point fields, Unicode, multiline layouts, deeper recursion
and effectful or shared objects remain required work.

### Ontology and taxonomy entry points

The library exposes ontology and taxonomy as separate entry points into the
same concept space, including concepts no grammar currently uses. The ontology
organizes concepts, relationships and formal axioms; the taxonomy organizes
classification and explicitly declared subsumption. General associative relations
remain separate from classification edges. This terminology follows the
[W3C ontology overview](https://www.w3.org/TR/owl2-overview/) and
[SKOS distinction between hierarchical and associative links](https://www.w3.org/TR/skos-reference/#semantic-relations).

A concept can participate in either entry point or both without losing its
global identity or formal definition. Each view can have several independently
selectable roots, all represented explicitly as links. A root may also be a type
when the selected model declares that role; root membership does not imply a
type role or a universal `Type : Type` axiom.
The current paired view APIs retain actual concept, recursive definition and
provenance links, including explicit orphan concepts. Ontology and taxonomy roots
are independently selectable; associations, axioms, classification and subsumption
carry declared model links. These declarations preserve assumptions and do not
establish arbitrary hierarchy or type entailment.
Paired acceptance controls construct views with and without a grammar base,
check both complete saved formats, and select every root against independent
declared traversal expectations. Separate membership selections retain the same
identity and definition where the views intersect, while allowing concepts to
belong to only one view. A taxonomy root can also carry an explicit archived
type model. Execution records follow all assertions; native records require CI.

### Links as fundamental entities and relationships

Concepts, entities, relationships, roots and definitions are all links or
compositions of links. The same link can occupy several of these roles without
becoming disconnected host objects. A vertex can be represented by a link whose
beginning and end refer to that same link; the self-reference and the chosen
model remain explicit. Recursive composition preserves shared references and
cycles across representation levels.

Paired foundation controls use the existing public link constructors to build
an explicit nineteen-link model: a doublet vertex, its selected model, three
role declarations sharing one identity, two linked root selections and a shared
motif with a mutual cycle. Complete text and binary archives retain every link,
endpoint, metadata field and term registration without the original network.
Independent endpoint, role, root and cycle corruptions fail the same checks.
The interned-point API retains its unary profile; doublet vertices use the
explicit selected model. These controls supply no project-wide formal meaning
equivalence, physical consistency or new logical axiom. Native execution remains
a CI requirement.

Optional type-root controls construct both untyped and explicitly typed roots
through the same public graph operations. The type role, root membership,
selected model and foundation premise are inspectable links; text and binary
reload preserve both complete choices. A doublet vertex self-loop introduces no
universe-membership relationship, universal self-typing axiom or proof logic.
Metadata alone cannot replace the explicit role and premise links in these
models. Corruptions of the model selection, premise, role or metadata fail the
complete graph oracle. Native execution remains a CI requirement.

The user's fractal and wave analogy is captured verbatim in the hashed
conversation sources as modelling motivation and a hypothesis. Physical claims
about waves and particles, mathematical self-similarity and absence of logical
contradictions are not established by this representation or analogy. Any
consistency or semantic equivalence claim requires its own explicit assumptions
and checked proof.

### Dynamic knowledge and language extension

Full RDF and OWL representation, import, conversion and merging are required
through the same links and concept space, retaining formal axioms, selected
semantic profiles, identities, provenance, conflicts and unresolved obligations.
Their semantics remain declared model choices.
The current structured RDF graph primitive represents terms, statements,
source-qualified blank nodes, occurrences, archived source graphs and checked
exact-term merge witnesses as inspectable links. Its selected RDF 1.1 model
retains literal lexical forms and original language-tag provenance; it does not
normalize datatype values. Full concrete-syntax import and export, unsupported
identifier forms and OWL reasoning remain required work.
The selected N-Triples syntax adapter imports concrete triples through shared
generated token, scalar and grammar decisions. Canonical output reads current
linked resource terms, while a separate syntax graph reconstructs historical raw
spelling and trivia. Explicit blank-node correspondences retain source scopes
through export and reimport. Unsupported identifier forms and full RDF syntax
coverage remain separate obligations; historical provenance cannot supply current
semantic output after the graph changes.

A shipped prepared snapshot is a starting point. Users can reconstruct the full
CST and concept space dynamically through link-cli or text and binary Links
Notation, then use the shared algorithms to extend meta-language with other
languages. Reload retains complete trees and formal definitions rather than a
token list or an opaque source envelope. Existing catalog behavior remains intact.
Full reconstruction records latency and resource use on pinned inputs, with the
representation, input size and execution environment attached to the measurements.
Lazy loading and token-only envelopes do not count as a measured complete rebuild;
the qualitative speed requirement does not invent a numeric deadline.
The complete-graph JavaScript and Rust archive drafts copy actual networks in two
passes, retaining cycles, disconnected members, original source identities,
exact registrations, selected syntax and allocator state. An explicit linked
descriptor selects the source groups and entry roots. Entries using the same
source network retain shared identities; independent networks keep separate
identities. Text and binary operations reload the whole graph eagerly, and
extensions select replacements and removals explicitly. Canonical text loading
is streamed under node, reference and byte limits and retains exact supported
parse flags. The public archive CLI packs, lists, selects and
extends these graphs. The dynamic session draft uses archived discovered
grammars and formal premises with the existing native compiler and common
concept verifier. Its default parse product is the complete concrete tree,
including mapped source planes and recursive grammar provenance. An optional
abstract projection retains that concrete product. Both runtimes retain private
linked session snapshots; exported views cannot mutate their grammar context.
Derived parser caches may be discarded without losing authoritative data.
Saved concrete products pin complete historical definition and configuration
roots, parser options and decorator records. Replacing a grammar creates a new
context while retaining older products and their source selections. Fingerprints
bind these exact contexts without asserting semantic equivalence. Snapshot
replacement is explicit, and structured encoders use the caller's finite budgets.
Shared archive and snapshot policies are generated automatically from JavaScript;
native adapters copy actual graph structures and invoke the existing compiler
and concept verifier. Formal definitions for the remaining concepts, language
specific semantic abstractions, link-cli workload integration and reconstruction
measurements remain implementation obligations. These drafts have no execution
evidence.
The dynamic `language` CLI delegates preparation, extension, parsing, inventory
and native grammar export to these same library operations in both packages.
Source grammars carry explicit source identity, language and edition; configuration
and formal premises enter as Links data. Parsing writes a complete archive with
CST as the default. Explicit AST selection keeps its original CST. Text and binary
reloads resolve recursive grammar references from the archive, and replacements
retain the pinned contexts of earlier documents. Shared source definitions own
option admission and projection selection; generated native adapters only transport
files, arguments and graph values. Rejections retain their diagnostics and a
nonzero exit code, including when a recovered concrete archive is available.
This command draft and its deferred controls do not establish catalog completeness,
reconstruction performance, link-cli integration or execution evidence.
The current binary graph primitive writes a versioned meta-language archive of
actual link identities, references, supported metadata, exact term-map keys,
selected syntax and the next allocation identity. Shared framing rules are
automatically translated into Rust. Its interoperable profile refuses unsafe
integer values and isolated surrogate strings before corruption. Legacy archives
can be read, but information absent from their format cannot be restored. This
graph primitive does not populate missing definitions, implement every link-cli
path or establish the required complete reconstruction performance measurements.
A separate binary metadata buffer candidate collects each record's metadata
before appending its length and payload, replacing the prior accumulated-output
prefix rewrite. The frozen prior source remains the comparison oracle;
production JavaScript rules and generated native rules retain their existing
bytes. Candidate generation preserves the original stale-style failure receipt,
authenticates all thirty-six historical replacement contexts and derives
separately checked styles from current complete declarations. Reuse requires an
exact declaration or a bijective bound-name correspondence. Changed bodies
receive fresh structural rules that guard namespace capture, actual borrowed
sibling calls and expression grouping. The explicit UTF-16 String flag is a
native generation input, not a serialized source model. All fifty-three checked
declarations and four preludes are retained in the candidate library. A separate
explicit CI oracle authenticates its five generated files, unchanged array
provider, wrapper and consumer, then compares seventy-four bounded invocations
covering both archive versions, every refusal code and offset, input
preservation and error precedence. Injected process contracts are distinct from
actual successful native compilation and execution. Production adoption,
whole-crate parity and performance improvement remain unverified.
Automatic grammar inference and discovery have the separately tracked
[issue #224](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-language/issues/224), with
modern primary research, concrete algorithms, pinned assumptions and independent
accepted and rejected observations. The issue records scope; it is not inference
implementation evidence.
The issue-tracking control authenticates its complete registered source, four
pinned research revisions and all ten acceptance paragraphs. The twelve separate
implementation obligations remain independent of that tracking observation.
The discovery integration adapter accepts explicit discovered grammar outputs
and copies their actual recursive formal archives into the global concept graph.
Reload preserves registered term lookups, rechecks identity certificates and
retains unresolved alternatives and source provenance. This primitive integrates
discovery outputs; it does not supply the complete inference engine or formal
definitions for every catalog concept.
The lexical inference, state merging, context-free discovery, structural priors,
active learning and minimization already present in the Rust inference modules
and their closed issues are audited and extended, with matching JavaScript
behavior. Closed issues do not supply current implementation evidence.

A new shared source draft supplies labelled prefix-tree construction, red/blue
RPNI selection, evidence-driven selection and an ALERGIA-style compatibility
worklist in both runtimes. First-observed symbol and state order explicitly
break ties. The compatibility bound uses a versioned, finite binary64 logarithm
series; it is not asserted identical to the older native implementation's
transcendental library decisions. Historical native APIs remain intact. The
algorithm follows the state-merging families described in the
[Abbadingo paper](https://mural.maynoothuniversity.ie/id/eprint/10250/) and
[Carrasco and Oncina's stochastic grammar paper](https://www.dlsi.ua.es/~carrasco/papers/RAIRO99.pdf).
Its public wrappers retain duplicate observations, contradictory-label refusal,
explicit limits and actual transition/final frequencies. Without a separate
lexer definition, token texts must be nonempty, well-formed and prefix-free;
Unicode character examples satisfy that condition. Missing transitions reject.
The resulting right-linear grammar uses the normal full CST parser path.

The complete typed algorithm, declared inputs, selected parameters, actual model,
recursive candidate grammar and common concept definitions serialize together
in a complete-network archive. Reload checks the exact versioned algorithm
definition and recomputes its model decisions before comparing the candidate
grammar. The source view can extend a dynamic language session; no language-pair
dispatch table is added. Training observations and this internal reconstruction
are not independent evidence of the unknown language's meaning. Learned
similarity never permits global identity deduplication without its separate
formal certificate. Worklist, merge and sample budgets return explicit linked
failure values rather than truncating inference silently. Native source is
generated automatically from JavaScript. Formatter repair and all behavioral
checks remain deferred under the current whole-scope draft instruction.
Context-free inference, active learning, semantic induction, lexical inference,
minimization and the modern research obligations still require the remaining
paired implementation work; these regular learners do not discharge them.

### Critical conversation requirements

Every requirement-bearing conversation message is captured verbatim, hashed,
registered and mapped to atomic ledger obligations. New messages append records;
they do not overwrite earlier requests. All conversation and inherited ledger
requirements are critical and block a full-scope delivery or release-completion
claim while required exact-commit evidence is missing. Recording a requirement,
a null implementation entry point or a contract capture does not prove behavior.
The existing pre-merge, post-merge and release checkpoint order remains intact.

The current implementation priority maximizes Rust ↔ editable meta-language and
JavaScript ↔ editable meta-language translation, reusing the same algorithms
across multiple real JavaScript and Rust projects. RDF, OWL, dynamic extension,
formal definitions and every earlier language remain required scope.
The [automation-first baseline instruction](../parity/sources/automation-first-own-codebase-baseline-2026-10-21.txt)
makes complete JavaScript and Rust parsing, source emission and translation in
both directions for this repository the first end-to-end baseline. Concrete
syntax remains the default; faithful reconstruction and executable semantic
translation remain separate checks. Other languages and capabilities follow
this baseline without losing any recorded obligation. Reuse the existing
self-translation inventory, checked source contexts, workers and reports.
Group actual failures by their retained causes, preserve every affected source
and range, and prioritize general fixes that cover many real declarations with
few changes. The requested twenty-percent effort for eighty-percent results is
an optimization target: report measured coverage and resource use rather than
assuming that ratio. Unsupported constructs remain explicit.
The critical-delivery tooling checks every current obligation's critical and
full-scope blocker flags, then tests a complete synthetic success control before
negative exact-commit, fixture/assertion coverage and scope-reduction controls at
every checkpoint. Synthetic gate inputs are never recorded as language behavior.
The original conversation fixture receives execution observations only after all
three declared assertions pass. Adding an implementation entry point does not
make the row pass without its exact-commit CI observations. The complete original
conversation module remains independently Git-certified with an exact byte
inverse; no inherited obligation or gate is replaced.
The authorized bulk workflow obtains the latest repository refs, uses the
maximum useful independent agent delegation, commits every contributor's owned
work, and reserves pushing for one coordinator. That coordinator verifies prior
CI is finished and every prior error has a concrete fix before the next reviewed
batch push. A feedback push does not finish the authorized work: the coordinator
continues implementation and CI follow-up until the required work is complete,
as reiterated in the [captured follow-through instruction](../parity/sources/ci-follow-through-2026-10-13.txt).
The [current bulk completion instruction](../parity/sources/continuous-bulk-completion-2026-10-19.txt)
requires all remaining changes to be drafted and delivered. Opening a continuation
PR is a checkpoint; implementation continues during CI under the same full scope.
Local validation uses targeted JavaScript checks and never compiles
Rust; compiled parity and expensive workloads belong to CI.
The [local resource instruction](../parity/sources/meta-language-local-resource-scope-2026-10-16.txt)
restricts cleanup to meta-language-owned disk space and requires local work to
respect available disk, memory and process capacity. Rust compilation is
exclusive to CI/CD. The [resource limits](#resource-limits) govern shared
checkout use and the prompt, verified release of completed task worktrees.
The [cancellation investigation instruction](../parity/sources/ci-cancellation-investigation-2026-10-16.txt)
requires evidence for the reported cancellation of run 38431880453. Its frozen
Links receipt retains actual run, job, check-run, annotation and historical
workflow responses with their hashes. The checker joins their identities and
timestamps before accepting the explicit ten-minute automatic-timeout annotation.
Missing or contradictory evidence leaves the cause unknown; actor metadata
does not identify who cancelled a run. The historical head configuration is
labelled separately from an unverified effective merge checkout. This finite
investigation preserves the obligation to finish and repair subsequent CI runs.
The instruction to gradually reduce agents remains a separate hashed source and
critical obligation for that historical coordination phase. Contributors finished
their existing work and the coordinator continued alone. The subsequent
[instruction to expand delegation](../parity/sources/expanded-agent-delegation-2026-10-13.txt)
authorized a later coordination phase: split useful independent work among additional
agents, retain clear ownership and contributor commits, and preserve one push
coordinator. This operational steering preserves every earlier project requirement.

### Associative data storage

Project-owned structured data uses inspectable links serialized in Links
Notation. This includes grammar fixtures and corpus expectations, concept
definitions, transformation rules, inventories and verification data.
Conversion is automated through the associative stack, preserving every record,
key, ordered occurrence, scalar value, identity, alias and assertion. Reload
reconstructs the same data; an opaque JSON string inside a link is not a
conversion to associative data.

JSON remains only where an actual standard, external interface or source-language
fixture requires it, with the reason recorded. Package manifests and standard
lockfiles, JSON source programs, and upstream JSON inputs downloaded temporarily
are such interfaces. The native grammar fixture corpus, including the Agda
corpus, uses the shared structured-data codec. Its typed links preserve ordered
object fields, array occurrences, exact string code units and numeric values.
Persisted observations use individually scoped link records, so concurrent
writers cannot reuse each other's node identities. CI verifies both runtimes.

The exact source exceptions and their consumers are recorded in
[`parity/project-data-json-exceptions.lino`](../parity/project-data-json-exceptions.lino).
Cleanup leases, the cleanup lock and temporary scratch markers retain their
existing JSON coordination protocols: older active readers need those records
to recognize live owners. Their registry entries identify each writer and
reader. Changing those formats requires upgrading every reader sharing that
state and finishing older active owners, or negotiating protocol fencing.
Explicit `clean-caches --json` requests retain their JSON output contract;
the tool's own report and history storage uses Links Notation.

Oversized grammar reports use bounded typed LiNo object fragments with a checksum
index. `readGrammarReport` reconstructs every original field, occurrence order and
repeated object identity. Each fragment retains the codec's existing depth, node
and byte limits. The native global concept snapshot remains a direct semantic
graph. Shipped concept records retain their frozen array interface while loading
and authenticating the complete register on first use; a generated count and
digest keep ordinary package imports within their existing memory budget.

Historical conversion checks authenticate the original Git JSON and the exact
committed conversion separately from current generator freshness. The earlier
Odin provider receipt adjustment has an explicit
[metadata correction record](../parity/fixtures/structured-data-migration-corrections.lino).
It authenticates the unchanged original fields and the provider digest against
the recorded later grammar snapshot; it does not claim that snapshot existed at
conversion time. Frozen migration hashes remain unchanged. Current generated
fixtures must pass their own registered checks before the conversion witness
can accept an updated output.
The same correction register records that the historical Pascal conversion
added `rootIncludesLeadingTrivia: true` before `matches`. Its receipt
authenticates the original Git value and a later immutable native grammar
inventory with the same grammar and projection metadata. Replay permits only
that absent Boolean field at its recorded position; every original field,
source, golden row, alias and occurrence order remains unchanged. The receipt
does not claim the later inventory existed at conversion time.

## Links Notation fitness

Links Notation (LiNo) is the serialization of the common language. Both packages
depend on the current links-notation release. The npm and crates.io releases are
checked separately rather than assumed to be at the same version. Version-coupled
grammar fixtures are regenerated against the release, and the installed packages
are tested.

A feature-by-feature compatibility matrix
([`parity/fixtures/lino-compatibility-matrix.lino`](../parity/fixtures/lino-compatibility-matrix.lino))
shows that LiNo plus meta-language's representation layer can encode, decode,
edit and reconstruct everything this document requires:

- named and anonymous links;
- arbitrary arity;
- shared, recursive and forward references;
- identity and ordering;
- indentation and nested multiline groups;
- quoting and escaping;
- comments and trivia;
- Unicode and exact source mappings.

Where the dependency's basic parser discards a distinction, for example
comments, blank lines or the spelling of a quote, the native lossless LiNo
grammar ([`js/src/lino-grammar.js`](../js/src/lino-grammar.js),
[`rust/src/lino_grammar.rs`](../rust/src/lino_grammar.rs)) keeps it in the
concrete syntax tree.

The upstream findings of
[relative-meta-logic](https://github.com/link-foundation/relative-meta-logic/pull/184)
are regression tests in both runtimes and are re-tested on every upgrade. They
cover:

- whitespace and runtime differences;
- indented identifiers;
- deep nesting;
- long quote runs;
- stack safety;
- nonlinear parsing.

Resource safeguards are explicit and tested. Nesting deeper than the official
64-level limit becomes an `ERROR` node, never a stack overflow. Parse cost is
guarded as linear in the input size. These safeguards are not disguised as
missing language support. If a required feature is missing from Links Notation,
meta-language implements the representation support, with tests, as part of the
delivery. An upstream ticket or a feature declaration alone does not count.

## Native merged grammars

The shipped canonical grammar of every language is a **native Links Notation
grammar**. It is synthesized by carefully merging every applicable inventoried
source: official grammars and specifications,
[ANTLR grammars-v4](https://github.com/antlr/grammars-v4), tree-sitter grammars
and other mature implementations. The following do not count as the native
implementation:

- selecting one convenient grammar;
- copying a generated parser;
- wrapping an external engine;
- storing a foreign grammar as a string;
- renaming imported files.

Ordinary installed parsing works without any of these:

- external grammar engines;
- subprocesses;
- foreign generated parsers or scanners;
- downloads;
- caller registration.

Any generated acceleration derives reproducibly from the native Links Notation
definitions and does not conceal another authoritative grammar. Importers and
converters, described in
[grammar import, conversion and reverse conversion](#grammar-import-conversion-and-reverse-conversion),
are authoring and interoperability tools. Their final product is a full native
grammar that meta-language itself can inspect, transform, execute and emit.

The **native dependency boundary** is tested. The installed production packages
parse with every external grammar engine and oracle asset absent. Changing one
native linked grammar rule changes the output of the public parse path, without
an unrelated host-code patch.

Natural-language documents also use executable native Links grammars. Their
concrete representation retains Unicode lexemes, punctuation, whitespace,
sentence and document structure, exact source spans and rejected-control
diagnostics. Language-specific lexical and grammatical annotations extend that
tree with links and provenance. An uncertain analysis remains a set of explicit
alternatives; a segmentation tree or a finite starter lexicon does not establish
complete morphology, syntax or meaning for a language. Their concepts belong to
the same global space as programming languages, and reuse requires the same
checked meaning correspondence. Tests distinguish native representation and
source preservation from any richer linguistic capability they verify.

## Grammar feature union

Grammar attributes preserve ordinary JavaScript own-key order: canonical
array-index names come first numerically, other names retain insertion order,
and overwrites retain position. The complete shared JavaScript kernel generates
native key decisions and every continuation. Native nodes retain order beside
their historical lookup values; public attributes use an ordered map, and
physical witnesses borrow the same entries without a full intermediate copy.
Compact rendering sorts names by UTF-16 code units in both packages. Historical
attribute equality still compares dictionary meanings. Shared grammar fixtures
retain decimal boundaries, overwrite values and Unicode names; the map's exact
dependency version selects a bounded assertion profile. Native execution remains
a CI check.

Structured scanner descriptors cover whole-line quoted and FIFO here-documents,
nested quoted and interpolation contexts, offside layout tokens and Markdown
fence width and indentation rules. Shared accepted and rejected lexical fixtures
verify their concrete trees in both executors. These scanner checks do not
establish completion of a whole language grammar or its independent corpora.

Reusable scanner family descriptors generate executable Links Notation data
through `js/scripts/scanner-families.mjs`. Delimiter parameters cover nested
comments, escaped strings and content that stops at interpolation boundaries.
Both executors consume the generated operations in
`parity/fixtures/scanner-families.lino` and compare complete concrete trees,
including Unicode text and rejections of truncated delimiters.
Repeated delimiter counts use the same operation language. The raw-string and
long-bracket cases in `parity/fixtures/scanner-counted-delimiters.lino` verify
matching marker counts, shorter closing candidates inside content, Unicode
bytes and independent state for successive tokens in both executors.
The split counted-delimiter family retains the opening count across separate
opening, content and closing tokens. The generated Lua scanner uses it for
long strings and block comments, with empty content and NUL rejection, and
reproduces the pinned upstream scanner's eight-bit counts. The native Lua
grammar and its corpus inputs are pinned alongside the other source grammars;
CI compares both executors with their independent Lua oracles.
The delimiter-run family shares configurable quote-run content and closing
operations; the line-boundary family emits a zero-width boundary before a
newline or the end of input. TOML uses both families, and
`parity/fixtures/scanner-delimiter-runs.lino` checks several delimiter lengths
and line endings with both executors.
The generated TOML, Zig, Pascal and Visual Basic grammars record reviewed
precedence, overlapping-choice and field-variant reconciliations in the import
pipeline. Contextual rule variants keep original concrete kinds and concept
identities while isolating overlapping loop-prefix expressions; opaque operand
rules retain their ordinary expression context. Optional literal preferences
retain the absent branch and consume the pinned parser's field modifier.
Alias-pattern priorities distinguish explicit conditional directives from generic
preprocessor extras. Field-selected associativity preserves each source alternative
while reproducing reference-led chained comparisons. Error-union priorities retain
optional and pointer type prefixes around their complete operand.
Shared operand precedence now requires evidence from the actual first child or
its inspectable reduction. A literal prefix cannot inherit an operand position
from another production alternative. Thirteen pinned Rust controls preserve
complete CST rows and field roles, and the affected Formal AI source reproduces
all 7,321 oracle rows without changing its source grammar or census. This checks
the imported grammar's concrete structure; it does not establish the compiler's
semantic interpretation of an ambiguous CST operator.
The CSS, PowerShell and Erlang imports use shared marked-lookahead, contextual
token and line-counted delimiter scanner families. Contextual keyword exclusions
retain every source production and reserve initial statement keywords while
preserving command prefixes, pipeline tails and explicit invocations. The pinned
Erlang scanner license accompanies its source separately from the grammar license.
CI reads immutable pinned corpus inputs, including Zig compiler
source cases and Visual Basic project sources, and compares every input with
its independent oracle in both runtimes.

The indentation family generates zero width newline, indent and dedent tokens
with descriptor owned widths, bounded counts, comments, continuation and reset
characters. The shared `parity/fixtures/scanner-indentation.lino` examples check
independently specified nested block structures and exact source preservation.
The executor retains actual lexed continuations across intervening virtual
tokens when deciding whether a pending layout token preempts a longer parse.
The prefixed quoted family stores raw, bytes and interpolation flags with each
quote delimiter and its triple quote policy. Python uses the indentation and
prefixed quoted families in its generated native grammar; ordinary parsing
selects that grammar in both packages. Focused examples retain complete oracle
trees for nested formatted strings, escapes, layout and exception aliases.
The pinned upstream corpus remains a separate CI check; this implementation
does not yet constitute the required multi source union of Python grammars.

The Python statement-boundary correction reuses the recorded
`rule-prefix-exclusion` policy to guard the silent `Python_statement` rule
against an immediately pending dedent. The speculative scanner state is
discarded; the owning block consumes its closing dedent. Separate pure CST
prefix walks and their index retain the original predicates, insertion order
and trace callbacks; indexing alone does not resolve Python scaling.
Pipeline generation retains 158 of the 159 rule bodies unchanged. Focused
complete-tree and losslessness controls and the original 16- and 128-unit
inputs pass in JavaScript; native timing, stack and Windows verification
remain pending CI.

The template context family retains nested quoted, interpolation, directive
and here-document contexts, with closing labels on a separate text stack.
The generated HCL grammar uses that family in both packages; Tree-sitter HCL
is a development oracle. Scanner `matched` reads from the token start through
the current cursor, independently of a marked token end. Wrapping a scanner
token retains its skipped trivia outside the token span; zero-width layout
tokens keep their grammar-owned padding. The shared catalog also records
whether a language's root includes leading trivia, so HCL retains its input
start while its child nodes keep their actual token boundaries. HCL collection
comprehensions are distinct from imperative loop expressions. Focused fixtures
check nested templates, Unicode escapes, closing-line boundaries and label
reset; CI checks the pinned upstream corpus. This single pinned HCL source
does not yet constitute the required multi source grammar union.
The language catalog generator also derives Rust's embedded grammar table
from the shared inventory, so every declared native grammar is available to
ordinary parsing without a separate host registration.

The grammar representation and its executor cover the complete union of the
grammar features that the inventoried sources use:

- ordered and unordered alternatives;
- recursion, including left recursion;
- precedence and associativity;
- ambiguity and conflicts;
- lexical priority and longest-match rules;
- Unicode and byte classes;
- token boundaries and trivia;
- lexer modes, channels and state;
- indentation and layout;
- context-sensitive predicates;
- semantic actions and attributes;
- captures, fields and aliases;
- parameterization;
- imports and inheritance;
- macros and notation;
- embedded languages;
- error and missing nodes, and recovery.

External scanner behavior and action semantics become structured, executable
link definitions. They are not uninterpreted source blobs or hidden
language-specific callbacks.

The native importer lowers character-class intersection, difference and
symmetric difference to the existing Links lookahead and consuming operations.
Ranges and implicit union bind before set operations, which associate to the
left; outer negation binds last. Both packages execute the generated Links
data, with positive and negative ASCII and Unicode fixtures, and an independent
regular expression oracle in tests.
Hexadecimal scalar escapes with fixed widths or braces lower to literal
characters and ranges through the same importer.

Every unique concept and semantic distinction is preserved. Concepts are shared
only where a faithful one-to-one correspondence of meaning is established:
similar spelling, or accepting the same strings, is not enough. In particular
the representation distinguishes:

- ordered from unordered choice;
- lexical from syntactic precedence;
- different integer and overflow models;
- binding rules;
- effects;
- proof universes.

## Concrete and abstract syntax trees

Default parsing produces a **full lossless concrete syntax tree**. It records:

- node kinds;
- fields;
- hierarchy and order;
- exact tokens, trivia and source bytes;
- spans;
- error and missing nodes with their recovery;
- embedded-language boundaries.

A source envelope, or a root node above a list of tokens, is not a complete tree.
Optional abstract syntax trees keep explicit provenance back to the concrete
syntax. Trees can be:

- serialized and reloaded without the original source;
- constructed;
- edited;
- generated back to source;
- reparsed.

Concrete syntax is the default for every supported language and every nested
region. Abstract syntax is an explicitly selected projection or mode with
provenance to the complete concrete tree; omitting that selection always keeps
the full concrete representation.

The paired generic AST draft projects complete structured CST graphs through
shared automatically translated membership policies. Every abstract node points
directly to its actual concrete object. Each child records the original concrete
container and reference position; embedding roots, physical source children and
all alternative roots retain distinct relationship roles. Original concrete links
and identities remain present. Default projection removes marked trivia while
retaining every other token, including operators, and every error/missing record.
Inspectable `cst-to-ast` decorators may explicitly change or drop projected
records. Their complete records and tree-selection path stay linked in the
artifact; dynamic sessions save concrete and abstract roots in the same source
group. No host callback supplies the default abstraction.

Projection readers accept editable abstract relationships with explicit source
provenance, check actual original child origins, reject cycles and preserve shared
node identities. Finite node/reference/byte, materialized-property and actual
depth limits cover reconstruction, including cached subgraphs. Exact UTF-16
property strings remain structured values. The native decorator host transports
each code unit bijectively to one private scalar before invoking the existing
equality/replacement algorithm, then restores the original units. This changes
no public source or stored decorator data. Native decorator declarations still
need the existing exact supported text profile; broader declared-value profiles
remain explicit work. These source drafts have no behavioral observations and
do not establish language-specific semantic abstraction or full translation.

An inspectable linked `any-grammar` or `all-grammar` selection rule invokes
supported grammar definitions recursively through the same algorithms and
global concept space. Embedding is defined by grammar data and explicit
language-selection policies, including their boundaries and ambiguity
diagnostics, rather than host-specific code for every language pair. The model
must support Markdown within Markdown and deeper chains such as Markdown →
JavaScript → JSON → Markdown → Python, with returns to an earlier language and
applications to mixed logs and documents. Every level retains its full tree,
source-qualified grammar and concept identities, delimiters, escape mappings,
trivia, host spans and translation provenance. Serialization and reload preserve
the same hierarchy. Non-consuming cycles and resource limits report diagnostics
instead of silently truncating embedded trees. Existing Markdown and HTML
region discovery alone does not establish this generic recursive behavior.

The bounded `json-string` selection profile maps one complete quoted JSON
string to Unicode scalar text. It retains the full physical host CST and the
full accepted child and alternative CSTs on their decoded source plane. A
mapped `parseTree` result carries one ordered `sourcePlanes` table; zero-based
local references identify actual derivations, independently of Links graph
identifiers or equal source text. Mapping intervals retain quote ownership,
escapes and covering physical spellings for decoded diagnostics. The existing
shared parse budgets and cycle checks apply across these planes. Identity-only
results keep their existing shape, while tree-only `parse` refuses mapped
success rather than discard its table. After generic Links decode, the mapped
CST checker rederives the actual bytes and validates source references, ordered
intervals and physical coverage. It does not prove language-grammar membership;
that requires the real grammar parse. Lone-surrogate decoded text and unknown
profiles refuse explicitly. Failed child parses may retain attempted source
derivations, but a result without a complete physical root cannot establish
mapped-CST ownership.

The explicit syntactic `included-ranges` profile retains that full physical CST
while concatenating ordered, disjoint UTF-8 byte ranges into a separate source
plane. Ranges are relative to the selected region, align with scalar boundaries
and must produce a proper byte contraction; all any/all candidates must declare
the same range parameters. Correspondence intervals retain excluded gaps and
scalar copies. Nested composition with `json-string` keeps each actual source
plane and its derivation. After text/binary Links reload, the mapped-CST checker
validates declared ranges and rederives bytes, intervals and child coverage
from the physical source.
The closed candidate and range forms admit exactly their declared data fields
and actual integer coordinates. Compilation, grammar serialization and global
concept archives refuse malformed records before copying, coercion or field
projection can discard their meaning. Archive definitions use inspectable data;
accessors and custom serialization cannot replace those definitions during
validation or export. Valid source-free archives retain the same concrete trees
and source mappings as their original grammars.
Mapping work uses existing shared parse budgets. Ranges are explicit grammar
data. The native compiler prepares its owned grammar closure
before input is available; range admission precedes execution of child parsers.
Native compilation and runtime parity remain subject to CI verification.

The finite `field-ranges` profile derives ranges from actual physical capture
fields. A `grammar-field-candidate` supplies a grammar name and a
`source-field-selector`: an exact label, `include` or `exclude`, zero-based
`first` and positive `count`. The whole occurrence window must exist; zero never
means all. Discovery follows topmost non-trivia matches in physical child order
and suppresses matching descendants. Identity embeddings are opaque to
selection; mapped embeddings expose only their physical `sourceChildren`.
The existing scalar-aligned contraction kernel consumes the resulting ranges
unchanged. Each completed source-plane derivation retains its immutable full
physical witness, selector and occurrences, including when its child parse
later refuses. Cache ownership follows actual parent children, rediscovering
current witness facts before reuse; distinct profiles retain independent
planes. After text/binary Links reload, the checker replays discovery without
original source files and checks correspondence, not grammar validity. Existing
shared budgets still apply. This finite discovery does not establish complete
Markdown or other priority grammars; scanner context and complete scanning
remain gaps. Public interfaces expose the selector, occurrences and physical
witness as distinct types. Native compilation and runtime parity require CI
verification.

The default CST adapter follows supported discovered child regions through a
FIFO queue, retaining direct-region order, actual parent Region links and
once-composed absolute UTF-8 spans. `ParseConfiguration` bounds child depth,
count and physical bytes at 128, 4,096 and 16,777,216 by default. Root parsing
is excluded; every admitted child parse attempt is charged, including
overlapping bytes. Legacy root aliases with a different source spelling retain
their one existing same-range parse; inner same-host, same-range views reuse
the attached CST without another boundary. Earlier-ancestor repetitions and
limit refusals retain the region boundary and a flagged diagnostic. These
adapter limits do not replace parser evaluation, recovery or memory budgets,
or establish generic any/all grammar selection, global concept-sharing proofs
or full grammar coverage. Native execution remains a CI obligation.

## Language catalog and default parsing

A versioned catalog lists the popular languages with their versions or editions,
dialects, aliases, extensions and embedded languages:

- [`parity/language-grammar-inventory.lino`](../parity/language-grammar-inventory.lino);
- the generated `js/src/data/language-catalog.lino` and
  `rust/src/data/language-catalog.lino`, which both runtimes dispatch through.

The catalog is reconciled with every existing dispatcher and every downstream
requirement. For each language it inventories the strongest available grammar
sources with pinned revisions, licenses, provenance and independent test corpora.
Every catalog language parses by default, through its alias and extension
dispatch and its embedded-language paths. Merely listing a language, keeping its
text or requiring caller registration is not default support.

Complete JavaScript, Markdown, Rust and TypeScript grammars are the initial
delivery priority, followed by the other languages used by these projects.
They produce full concrete syntax by default and are exercised on the projects'
own source files with independent accepted and rejected observations. This
supports reusable tools and automated translation of our own code through
editable links. A registered grammar, partial syntax subset, successful CST
parse or carried source alone does not establish complete executable semantic
translation. All earlier catalog and translation obligations remain required.

The bulk pipeline schedules these four languages first using the shared Links
Notation policy in `parity/grammar-work-priorities.lino`. Work still covers
every selected catalog occurrence under the same worker count and limits, and
results retain their original catalog positions. Earlier scheduling provides
earlier feedback; it does not establish complete grammar support.
CI publishes the textual matrix summary as a separate small artifact alongside
the complete generated grammar archive, allowing bounded feedback inspection.
Resource rejection descriptions retain their actual budget values and include
source coordinates only when the parser supplies them. Diagnostic rendering
does not change parser limits or establish grammar support.

Every catalog grammar occurrence is audited, including Markdown's block and
inline components and TypeScript's TSX component. Component roots retain their
source-qualified definitions in the global space; they are not interchangeable
alternative grammars to merge. Keep the original language sample and oracle
comparison, and identify additional document probes separately from actual
component composition. Missing, repeated and unstarted occurrences remain
visible in the audit.

CI reuses the pinned Markdown crate archive to authenticate actual grammar,
scanner, include, parser and corpus member hashes. External sources stay
temporary; Links receipts retain provenance. A source profile or capture cannot
establish executable scanner behavior. Source-free range and reinsertion rules
preserve byte ownership and original fields; native discontiguous parsing and
component projection must also work before composed CST support is claimed.

The inline delimiter candidate generates all fifteen source token identities
as shared Links operations. Its raw probes supply the complete simultaneous
source-symbol context explicitly, preserve the four serialized state bytes and
distinguish the inspected cursor from the marked token end. CI compares those
operations against the authenticated original C scanner, including wrapping
delimiter counts, continuation state and source punctuation rules. These finite
callback comparisons do not establish parser eligibility, Rust execution or
complete Markdown CST support. Keep native promotion blocked until the parser
supplies faithful context and the block scanner and composition also work.

The restricted C source-lowering experiment follows original declared helper
calls from the block scanner's fence entry point and archives typed instructions
with the full source CST. Enum values and variable references retain their actual
declaration owners as shared links; text and binary reloads preserve those
relationships, and replay rejects substituted equal-looking owners. CI reuses the
authenticated source archive and capture receipt; external source files remain
temporary. The supplied CST and declared ABI are explicit inputs. Internal replay
does not prove grammar derivation, memory semantics or executable C translation;
unresolved obligations remain data and the checked portable program remains absent.
The whole CST and declaration relationships remain in the archive. Encoding keeps
the codec's normal physical-node, depth and byte bounds. The authenticated CI
input declares a source-sized expanded-traversal ceiling and records measured
physical and expanded costs before replay; the reader takes an explicit caller
budget. Default refusal and every source/owner replay check remain required.

Source `sizeof` operations retain their actual operand CST, declaration owners,
selected ABI and unsigned result-type basis as linked layout queries. Their byte
size remains unresolved until object representation and layout are checked.
The original lowerer remains intact; a size-query adapter records source-bound
namespace refinements separately from the parser's selected type/expression
shape. A visible value binding takes precedence over a type spelling, and a
later or ambiguous declaration cannot supply the missing owner. Supported fixed
object operands remain unevaluated; array extents, possibly variably modified
types, unknown expressions and aggregate layout retain explicit obligations.
No operand call or update executes during this source-only type derivation.
Complete original source and test inputs remain available through reversible
Links publication. This does not repair the public C parser's ambiguity choices
or establish executable scanner translation.

Simultaneous scanner eligibility belongs to one explicit parser continuation,
including its caller returns, input range, scanner state and recovery phase.
Close that continuation to its token boundaries before constructing an immutable
validity vector. Keep diagnostic expectation sets in their existing role; their
accumulated requests do not establish this frontier. Retain an emitted symbol
independently of the initiating request, with its token extent, inspected cursor
and resulting state. Native promotion requires correspondence with the source
parser's actual eligibility contexts and independent complete CST comparisons.

Default native CST adapters must transport the complete selection result before
mapped composition is promoted into the catalog. Their current ordinary-tree
projection does not yet preserve embedded roots, physical `sourceChildren`,
mapping provenance and the `sourcePlanes` table in the public language CST.
Retain every existing ordinary CST contract while adding that transport in both
packages; successful generic archive parsing alone does not establish this path.

The bounded four-language archive controls keep complete C, JavaScript, Rust
and TypeScript grammar definitions together in one space, remove the original
grammar maps, and compare full qualified outcomes and independent CST rows after
reload. Their selected sources come from existing pinned fixtures; they do not
establish whole-language coverage. JavaScript also compares every public default
CST node and physical token on these inputs. Native execution remains a CI check.

The JavaScript-first [owned outcome experiment](../experiments/grammar-outcome-network/README.md)
uses the public parser registry to attach an entire source-free mapped result
to an actual default concrete source network. An explicit association names the
concrete owner, ordered physical tokens, structured-data root and every owned
graph member. Reload isolates that closed graph, checks its mapping derivations
and binds its physical bytes to the host tokens. Token identities must belong
to the actual owner's Syntax/Document containment graph, with the codec's node
bound also limiting that traversal; equal foreign bytes cannot prove ownership.
This complete-root profile requires physical coordinates beginning at zero.
Physical extraction traverses iteratively under the codec's node and byte
bounds and concatenates leaf bytes once. Oversized payload manifests refuse
before member cloning; expanded structured values retain the decoder's earlier
resource checks. This avoids repeated complete-source copies at nested nodes.
Ordinary UTF-8 results retain an absent source-plane field. Their checker uses
a temporary root-only envelope derived from actual owned bytes; mapped results
still require their complete source declarations and derivations.
Both accepted and rejected ambiguous results retain their complete trees,
alternatives, diagnostics and source planes. Results without a concrete tree
remain outside the experiment. Decoded-plane text stays within
the owned graph. The experimental concrete view includes all its members;
public default projection and native integration remain the gaps above.

The separate [native outcome transport experiment](../experiments/grammar-outcome-network/native-transport/README.md)
derives exhaustive borrowed visitors from the actual concrete syntax of all nine
native outcome declarations and an inspectable Links wire policy. Its proposed
encoder counts expanded occurrences, depth and scalar bytes before allocating
the graph. The original number conversion is extracted from its actual source
owner with a complete parent inverse. A CI-only scratch overlay keeps an explicit
frozen source baseline separate from the current checkout and compares ordered
values, binary64 bits and each native graph's text/binary replay with fresh
JavaScript parser results and independent constructed boundaries. Registered
external oracle inputs remain authenticated temporary downloads. Native execution
must precede any evidence claim. The finite candidate rejects depth limits above
256; arbitrary-depth safety, physical container-alias equivalence, a paired public
API and public default CST integration remain open. The existing generic codec's
limits and number semantics remain unchanged.

Diagnostic field order is observable in this transport. A source-derived Links
decorator corrects two actual native diagnostic producers to match the existing
JavaScript construction order, preserving every key, value expression and
condition. Current production sources and authenticated historical sources are
separate views with complete source inverses. The frozen CI overlay applies the
same correction only after checking its original bytes and records that role
explicitly; historical pins never become claims about current source. Dependency
assertions select the exact locked JSON serializer patch and declared ordered-map
feature, preserve their source hashes and report unsupported profiles. This
finite correction does not establish the missing inverse transport.

Its source-type inventory retains the complete original declarations and exact
type CSTs before wire projection. A closed reference graph preserves recursive
owners, every field and variant, qualified bindings, owning containers and the
distinction between `String` and `&'static str`. Shared Links policy derives from
authenticated authoring literals; regeneration preserves whole-source inverses,
and candidate projections must match the complete authenticated inventory.
Unicode type comments retain UTF-8 byte spans; unpaired UTF-16 input refuses.
Native target widths, effective JSON representation profiles, static-reference
resolution and original DTO reconstruction remain explicit obligations. This
inventory does not implement the paired decoder or public default CST transport.

The source-derived ordinary inverse reconstructs an owned occurrence graph from
the closed Links wire graph. It retains source field order, wrappers, qualified
variants, ordered map entries, option presence, integer bits and unresolved
static-borrow descriptors. Counting precedes construction; a separate validator
checks the whole graph before deriving controls for the existing forward visitor.
The complete ordered projection must equal the original input. Plans and owned
models use normal Links data, with text and binary reload controls. This finite
profile admits four ordinary syntax variants, null-only opaque JSON and explicit
32/64-bit logical pointer widths. A separate authenticated recursive profile
also owns Embed and nested JSON projections, preserving every scalar and ordered
member. Explicit occurrence links retain shared projected JSON containers;
independent equal containers stay distinct. Complete earlier subtrees and every
nested alias edge are checked before reuse; ancestor cycles refuse. Other native
owning container aliases retain their earlier refusal. JSON Numbers retain binary64 bits and explicit unresolved native variant
requirements; the erased signed/unsigned/float origin is never guessed. Both
profiles use the complete automatically translated portable decision module.
Native JSON representation facts, static-reference resolution, native DTO
reconstruction and physical CST integration remain required. JavaScript controls
do not prove native execution.

Owned occurrence topology has a separate complete shared worklist algorithm.
It checks the full ordered offset and child inventories, unique preorder
ownership, reachability and node, edge and depth reservations. Both runtimes
use the checked JavaScript translation, including every lifted control
declaration; the native control functions retain actual loops. The worklist
has explicit bounds and constructs no native DTO. Independent structural
controls and the actual fourteen owned projection models exercise this layer.
Native execution, source types, scalar representation and physical ownership
remain independent checks.

Complete source-type edge admission is also a shared translated algorithm. Its
schema derives from all nine original declarations, thirteen source pins and
every reachable type owner. Fixed fields, options, vectors, ordered map pairs,
qualified variants and recursive JSON children keep their exact source-type
identities and order. The captured ordinary profile retains its earlier Embed
and non-null JSON refusals. A caller's numeric schema cannot authenticate itself;
the JavaScript adapter first validates the complete owned model against the
captured source plan. All sixteen lowered declarations remain in generated
Rust, including seven actual iterative control functions. Independent shape
cases, every original projection, nonfinite columns and deep bounded chains
check this layer. Native execution, root and scalar admission, effective native
JSON variants, static references, original DTO construction and physical CST
ownership remain separate obligations. Edge admission supplies no completed
native transport or public CST integration claim.

Owned integer range admission uses one complete automatically translated
JavaScript operation. It admits exactly the binary64 integers within the signed
64-bit or active unsigned 32/64-bit range, excluding negative zero, nonfinite
values, fractions and unknown target widths. The original complete BigInt
operation remains an authenticated executable oracle in
[`owned-integer-range-source-history.lino`](../parity/naming/owned-integer-range-source-history.lino).
Both logical widths and immediate binary64 endpoint neighbors have shared native
fixtures. JavaScript reconstruction retains its original input classification,
exact decimal reconstruction and bit encoding. Whole-source inversion preserves
the complete preceding module. This range layer does not resolve native JSON
number origins, static references, physical ownership or actual DTO construction;
native execution remains a CI check.

Generated Option reads normalize pure floating literal defaults through recorded
Links source styles. The author authenticates the complete array helper and its
standard type context, then retains the complete owning functions and their
source inverses. Calls, allocated defaults, comments, custom types, imports,
extension traits, opaque macros and nested namespaces retain their original
forms. Eager evaluation applies only to an effect-free typed literal, preserving
the receiver evaluation, value and signed zero. This supplies no replacement
native algorithm and changes no lint gate. Both complete owned-outcome libraries
regenerate through this author.

Implicit module reference selection respects the complete frontend's checked
owned array rewrites before forcing reference representation from method syntax.
Explicit reference requests and imported reference signatures retain their
admission. A refused complete parse preserves lexical fallback. The complete
worklist source exercises this translation path without carried items and with
exact source restoration; native execution remains a CI requirement.

The CI overlay derives an additive pointer-width observation inside the actual
post-assertion probe report, retaining every original source byte through its
inverse. Compiler feature selection binds the probe's verbose `rustc --extern`
argument to exactly one actual Cargo compiler artifact, rather than combining
different feature units of the same dependency. The receipt records complete
stream hashes, selected artifact metadata and the executed primitive width.
The current format profile selects Cargo and the actually invoked Rust compiler
`>=1.90.0 <1.100.0` and the locked JSON
dependency `>=1.0.151 <1.0.152`; uncovered versions and ambiguous bindings refuse.
These observations require successful CI execution. Historical compiler logs and
synthetic selectors establish source and transport controls only; number variant
reconstruction and native DTO equality remain separate obligations.

Native number representation selection now has one complete JavaScript decision
module and its automatically generated Rust library. Its explicit model covers
`serde_json >=1.0.151 <1.0.152` without `arbitrary_precision`; unknown profiles
and contradictory native predicates refuse. Positive integers may satisfy both
signed and unsigned admission, and retain the unsigned representation. A separate
adapter binds this selection to the exact checked compiler artifact and refuses
its unsupported precision feature without combining unrelated feature units.
The 160 declared-profile controls, lossless source round trip and source-free
Links text/binary reloads establish selector behavior only. They do not capture
an original native number, authenticate caller-supplied logs as a live execution,
or reconstruct an owned DTO. Exact decimals, floating bits and source occurrence
ownership must still be obtained before projection. Native execution remains CI
work. Primitive Boolean source styles simplify only immutable primitive
parameters under their actual types, retaining the complete generated owner and
recorded inverse; unsupported expressions remain unchanged and shadowing refuses.

Exact native integer projection admission also uses a complete JavaScript
algorithm and its unchanged canonical-decimal dependency. Bounded decimal digit
division identifies the lowest and highest nonzero binary positions before
admitting a binary64 value. Range, lost low bits, a different projected value,
negative zero and uncovered widths refuse. Both complete source modules and
their one exact import correspondence remain in the generated Links fixture;
every lifted native control declaration is retained. Independent BigInt boundary
and seeded controls, exact source restoration and both typed archive encodings
check this layer. Native execution remains a CI requirement.

The experimental number-contract adapter first validates the complete owned
source model and selected compiler artifact, then binds one explicit supplied
fact to every unresolved JSON number occurrence in its original order. Integer
payloads retain their exact decimal and floating payloads retain their exact
bits; equal projections cannot erase distinct declared native variants. Missing,
foreign, rounded or changed contracts and active inputs refuse. The complete
selection reloads from text and binary Links. Supplied facts and historical
compiler contracts do not establish original-object capture or live execution
provenance. Native fact production, complete DTO reconstruction and default CST
integration remain required; these contracts do not change the owned model's
unresolved manifest or count as completed reconstruction.

The original-number observation prototype derives the complete borrowed visitor
for all nine captured native DTO declarations and preserves the original
allocation-free counter, formatter and integer projection validation. Its first
complete preflight retains the original immutable values; a second borrowed pass
records primitive Number predicates, exact integer decimals, floating bits and
expanded wire occurrence positions. Only an original JSON Number uses this
observation route. Host integer conversions retain their original operation.
Recorded Links decorators and whole-source inverses describe the extension.
Native CST intervals select UTF-8 bytes from a fully validated passive source
buffer. Both endpoints must be scalar boundaries; invalid surrounding bytes,
shared buffers, proxies and out-of-budget sources refuse before selection.
Unicode source remains exact without normalization. The complete preceding
author and receipt retain independent Git membership certificates and exact
byte inverses; generated native algorithms and numeric controls remain unchanged.
The new isolated profile requires explicit limits of at most 100,000 occurrences,
16 MiB scalar bytes and depth 256; existing codec defaults do not change.
Independent controls retain signed/unsigned overlap, wide exact integers,
negative zero and subnormals. Registered native tests cover every JSON-owning DTO
field, recursion, equal independent occurrences and refusals. Local source and
archive checks supply no native execution evidence. Selected compiler provenance,
the correspondence from wire positions to owned source identities and complete
DTO reconstruction remain required before integrating original observations
with the supplied-number contract or public default CST path.

The experimental number correspondence rechecks the complete owned model and
its independent wire projection, then follows the actual source field rules
and paths to join each JSON number site to its expanded wire ordinal. Equal
values, shared projected containers and host integer fields do not replace
source occurrences. Raw primitive records pass descriptor checks before any
supplied values are read. Exact signed/unsigned predicate overlap, both integer
decimals, floating bits and the selected compiler contract remain checked.
The complete result reloads from text and binary Links. Its finite route profile
admits at most 100,000 owned nodes, one MiB per route and sixteen MiB of total
route bytes; excessive routes refuse without reducing the original owned-model
profile. This is a checked contract over supplied records, without attestation
of their live origin. Native compiler provenance, runner publication and the
complete native DTO/default CST path remain required.

The current native control test publishes all seven fields directly from each
original borrowed number observation after its unchanged independent assertions.
The publisher is generated from the actual observation declaration, and emits
one bounded namespaced Links record on locked standard error only in the actual
CI evidence profile. It records the executed commit/run/attempt, actual pointer
width and compiler-embedded byte hashes of the test, observer, visitor, publisher,
control fixture and lock file. The complete original test module has an
authenticated Git certificate and an exact additive source inverse.
The retained-log reader checks the complete standard output/error hashes,
recomputes every native outcome, verifies the successful physical receipt and
selected test, and compares every original primitive control. Stale identities,
source changes, duplicate/incomplete records, ignored/failed tests and changed
number payloads refuse. Supplied matching logs remain a transport contract;
authentic CI origin, the existing complete physical union, selected compiler
feature units and full native model/default CST reconstruction remain required.
No synthetic transport control is published as a ledger observation.
After a successful actual physical execution, the runner reads its exact retained
receipt and complete logs with bounded fatal UTF-8 decoding, then writes the
checked Links value alongside them as an observation log. The artifact cannot
overwrite a previous result. It adds no receipt entry or nested physical cell;
the existing raw-output hashes and complete seal admission remain unchanged.
Other profiles and unselected tests retain their original execution paths.

The experimental typed-storage generator derives every constructor from all nine
captured native declarations and 120 independently qualified source type
occurrences. Shared Links instructions retain each original field, variant,
child type, alias, option and container. Native packing consumes actual typed
values; it preserves an original JSON Number and a caller-supplied existing
static reference without guessing either from its projected value. Reusing a
native storage carrier does not merge source concepts. Missing, foreign,
reordered and extra children, invalid variant choices and duplicate map keys
refuse. Generated source bindings are checked against the complete instructions,
including a field-swap mutation. Declared records and variants use boxed carriers;
collections, source boxes and primitives retain their direct native storage.
These are host adapters, without a complete owned-model validator, recursive
JSON decoder, live compiler attestation or public default CST integration.
Those remain required before claiming a complete inverse.

## Grammar import, conversion and reverse conversion

Both runtime packages provide public APIs and command-line tools to import,
validate, convert, merge, rename, export and round-trip grammars. The format
catalog starts with:

- BNF, EBNF and ABNF;
- PEG, Peggy and Pest;
- ANTLR;
- tree-sitter source and JSON;
- Lark;
- GBNF;
- Yacc/Bison;
- lexer specifications.

This list is a minimum and is reconciled with the other widely used formats.

The JavaScript Yacc/Lex draft lowers declared token rules, left and right
precedence, `%prec`, lexical modes, trivia and supported arithmetic or textual
actions to shared native Links operations. Rejection actions become explicit
failures. Unsupported prologues, arbitrary host actions, typed unions and other
unimplemented constructs retain precise diagnostics. These finite supported
cases do not establish complete source-format coverage, a Rust importer or the
union of the pinned official Ruby, Perl, Bash and OCaml grammars.

Grammar Links retain ordered lexer declarations, modes, owners, fragments and
bindings alongside productions. Source-free reload preserves their complete
declaration expressions, including unresolved action and predicate obligations;
serialization alone does not discharge those executable meanings.

ANTLR parser token exclusions consume one whole elected token; lexer
complements retain their character-set semantics. Excluding EOF still requires
consuming a non-EOF token. Lookahead does not commit lexer-mode changes;
consumption applies the elected token's commands once. A pure shared JavaScript
classifier supplies automatically translated native decisions. Text and binary
replay retain these expressions and their unresolved foreign obligations;
case-insensitive parser literals retain their existing refusal. Source-free
emission for one default-mode lexer recovers commands while retaining original
ordered local bodies; retyping aliases never replace actual lexer definitions.
The shared inverse requires exact replay of final token fields and ordered
actions. Generated policy refuses ambiguous ownership and unsupported modes or
sections when command recovery is required; historical raw-document lossy paths
remain when it is unnecessary. Native execution remains unverified. These
bounded changes do not establish a full mode emitter or complete language
support.

- Every feature of every inventoried format has an explicit native
  representation, an operational meaning and executable tests. Imports follow
  included grammars, supporting lexer and scanner definitions, and action code;
  importing only rule skeletons is incomplete. Grammar-level comments, source
  locations, original names and formatting provenance are preserved.
- Conversion runs **back** to every source format. The tested cycle is source
  grammar → native links → exported grammar → native links, with the original
  source unavailable to the emitter. It checks structural and semantic
  equivalence, modulo documented canonical names and reversible provenance
  mappings. A lossless mode additionally reconstructs the source exactly.
- Cross-format conversions and native → external → native cycles are tested
  alongside same-format cycles. Exported grammars run under the actual
  independent target tools, which compare:
  - accepted and rejected programs;
  - parse structure;
  - lexical decisions;
  - precedence;
  - actions;
  - diagnostics.
- Where a target notation is less expressive, the export is a faithful lowering
  or runtime encoding, with any reconstruction metadata kept explicitly. An
  executable target grammar is distinguished from a lossless interchange package.
  None of the following is proof of semantic preservation:
  - an opaque sidecar;
  - a silently dropped feature;
  - a syntactically valid file;
  - a finite membership sample.
- Round-trip tests change the grammar before export, so returning the saved input
  cannot pass. Malformed and negative cases stop two mutually wrong importers and
  exporters from validating each other.

## Automatic merging, concept recognition, deduplication and renaming

The default merge pipeline is non-interactive. It:

1. discovers and imports the inventoried sources;
2. recognizes corresponding concepts even when their names differ;
3. reconciles their definitions;
4. normalizes names;
5. deduplicates genuinely equivalent concepts and preserves unique ones;
6. emits the canonical native grammar together with a reproducible provenance
   and decision report.

Concatenating rules, prefixing every source namespace or asking users for every
correspondence does not satisfy this.

- **Renaming is binding-aware.** It runs through references, imports, captures,
  semantic actions, mappings and generated artifacts, and keeps stable
  identities and the original source aliases for export. Tests cover:
  - collisions;
  - shadowing;
  - recursive and mutually recursive rules;
  - qualified references;
  - references after serialization and reload.
- **Duplicates are recognized by meaning**:
  - meaning-aware normalization;
  - recursive graph and alpha-equivalence;
  - lexical and parser semantics;
  - justified equivalence mappings.

  Name similarity and generated examples only nominate candidates. Tests cover
  differently named equivalent concepts and identically named non-equivalent
  concepts.
- **Uncertain matches are not conflated.** Distinct meanings, and version,
  dialect or ambiguity alternatives, stay explicit. An unresolved required
  equivalence is failing work, not a request for maintainer feedback or a
  permanent manual mode. Finitely many samples never prove arbitrary semantic
  equivalence.
- **Shipped and test grammars are merge outputs.** Both the production grammars
  and the native grammars used in tests come out of the multi-source merge.
  Upstream oracles stay independent. Each compatible source keeps its unique
  valid coverage. Dialects are not united indiscriminately, and disagreements
  are not erased to improve a score.
- **Merging is deterministic.** Tests cover:
  - reproducible output and idempotence;
  - stability under input reordering;
  - incremental re-merging after upstream changes;
  - conflict resolution;
  - no duplicate canonical identities for established equivalences;
  - preserved language and edition boundaries.

  Explicit precedence between sources is a reproducible rule justified by
  language specifications and independent evidence.
- **"Best possible" is measured.** The order of importance is:
  1. construct coverage and correctness;
  2. preserved unique features and shared reuse;
  3. measured recovery quality, execution time and memory.

  Comparisons against the inventoried source grammars are published.
  Superiority is never claimed from passing the same self-generated fixtures.

## Readable English names
