to their selected targets, with explicit refusals elsewhere. This connects the
existing backend implementations; it does not claim new support for previously
unsupported state, ownership or dependent-language constructs. Future controls
cover readonly collections, four-target codec replay, paired source emission
and one native product. No such control has run in the complete draft phase.

The RDF identifier draft also composes a source-generated IP-literal grammar
with the existing scheme, Unicode, userinfo, port, path, query and fragment
rules. It follows [RFC 3987 section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3987#section-2.2)
and its [RFC 3986 address productions](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.2.2):
full and compressed IPv6, an optional final IPv4 tail, and IPvFuture version
and address syntax. The temporary host projection is only a validation premise;
the actual linked IRI and every source token retain their original spelling.
No address normalization or dereferencing is performed. Zone identifiers and
other extensions outside this selected RFC grammar do not become admitted by
accepting arbitrary bracketed text. The frozen original lexical kernel remains
unchanged; the additional grammar is a separate complete typed Links definition.
Graph and N-Triples adapters consume the same generated decisions in both
packages. Earlier unsupported-address expectations need their explicit profile
reconciliation in the deferred metadata/assertion batch, retaining all inputs
and evidence. Future normative, codec and exact runtime-graph controls have been
authored. Formatter repair and behavioral checks remain deferred; this expansion
does not establish complete RDF/OWL/import/conversion support.

CI keeps its pinned Lean version when the upstream release index is
unavailable. A Linux x64 GitHub Actions fallback downloads the same official
release from GitHub, checks its published SHA-256 before extraction, and checks
the binary version before selecting it. Download, checksum and version failures
remain fatal, and the fallback refuses local execution. The selected Elan home
persists into later evidence steps so its shims resolve the same toolchain.

Self-translation representation selection uses shared linked decisions and an
automatically generated native implementation. Its selected UTF-16 prelude is
extracted from the existing JavaScript emitter without rewriting that runtime.
The native context carries this explicit selection while preserving ordinary
module context and constants emission. Selected signatures that still need
checked Text lowering retain an explicit obligation; emitting a String prelude
alone does not prove UTF-16 value semantics in Rust.

`charCodeAt` lowers the complete portable `readStringCodeUnit` function through
parsing, checking and shared Links data into a closed lambda using existing
callable, binding, Math, UTF-16 slice and code-point operations. It adds no
checked-method enumeration or target runtime helper and keeps the historical
UTF-16 runtime unchanged. The checked application evaluates receiver and index
once, in source order. Represented Number indices truncate toward zero;
omitted, null, undefined and NaN indices select zero, while out-of-bounds
positions and infinities return NaN. Runtime string parameters retain isolated
surrogates and each half of a surrogate pair. Boolean, String and object index
conversion, escaped lone-surrogate source literals and standalone Absence
annotations remain explicit interpretation gaps. Lean and Rocq retain their
UTF-16 operational-encoding refusals. Generic native callable ownership may
clone the complete Text argument; this gives no constant-time or allocation-free
guarantee. Full emitted-Rust runtime reimport and bidirectional execution remain
separate obligations; native execution requires CI verification.

`charAt` uses the same closed-source mechanism with the complete
`readStringCharacter` definition. The existing packaged Links policy appends its
independently checked character template; removing that extension reconstructs
every preceding code-unit policy byte. Both checkers instantiate the same
ordinary lambda, preserving one UTF-16 unit, empty out-of-range results, numeric
index normalization, caller spans and once-only operand evaluation. Text and
binary program archives retain these operations without a new graph operation
or handwritten native character algorithm. The represented numeric conversion
and source-literal boundaries above still apply. This follows
[ECMAScript character selection](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/text-processing.html#sec-string.prototype.charat).

Exact source integer reconstruction also retains canonical decimal text before
binary64 projection. Shared JavaScript decisions select signed 64-bit and
explicit 32/64-bit unsigned limits and validate at most twenty UTF-16 units with
an iterative decimal digit traversal. Complete automatic native translation
retains every lifted control declaration. Independent BigInt and native standard
integer parsing check canonical spelling and exact endpoints, including distinct
integers that share a binary64 value. The complete earlier ordering draft and
its original unsupported string-order diagnostic remain an executable historical
fixture. The complete original draft now translates without carried items and
restores every source byte using the explicit JavaScript ordering model below.
Effective native JSON number origins, complete DTO construction, static borrows
and physical CST ownership remain required; decimal admission alone does not
discharge them.

JavaScript `Number.isInteger`, `Number.isSafeInteger`, `Number.isFinite` and
`Number.isNaN` inspect the represented value without numeric conversion. Shared
Links decisions distinguish Number, optional Number and known nonnumeric types.
Missing arguments yield false; all supplied expressions still evaluate once in
source order, including ignored extra arguments. Both checkers construct existing
numeric, optional and binding nodes from the same automatically translated
rules. Eighty independent Node primitive observations retain their actual source
expressions. Complete text and binary archives recheck the selected behavior.
Boxed primitives, symbols, unresolved unions, unsupported empty-container
inference and iterable argument expansion remain separate obligations. Ordinary
Math conversion boundaries retain their previous diagnostics. The model follows
[the ECMAScript Number predicates](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/numbers-and-dates.html#sec-number.isfinite).

JavaScript primitive string relations `<`, `<=`, `>` and `>=` retain explicit
UTF-16 code-unit lexical meanings in the shared typed Links schema. Complete
shared JavaScript selectors and rendering rules generate every native declaration.
Rust targets use the actual `ml_string::Text` type through a fully qualified
comparison trait, preserving isolated surrogates and supplementary characters
whose order differs from UTF-8 or Unicode scalar order. Ordered operands evaluate
once. Complete text and binary program reloads retain the selected model and
recheck before emission. Independent primitive observations cover 2,916 string
pair relations and every individual UTF-16 unit. Ordinary Rust `String` source
ordering remains a distinct unsupported source model; Lean and Rocq refuse the
missing UTF-16 operational encoding. The original schemas, rule meanings and
style prefix retain complete historical source identities. Native execution
and full Rust runtime reimport remain CI and translation obligations.

Self-translation also retains its existing authenticated source envelope when
leading or trailing whitespace or inter-group gaps cannot be reconstructed by
emitted block separators, including minified adjacent items. A complete shared
JavaScript layout decision generates the native rule. Exact original UTF-8
bytes, language and source digest still require the hash of the entire actual
translated body before restoration. Editing emitted code cannot restore obsolete
source. This source-preservation metadata does not count carried items as
executable translation or substitute for the complete CST contract.

Owned native reconstruction can separately plan static string-reference
selection from a caller-supplied existing registry. It authenticates the complete
owned model and actual captured static reference type before reading occurrence
values. Shared JavaScript selects one unique label with explicit entry and UTF-8
byte bounds and generates every native control declaration. Duplicate labels,
missing values, active inputs and exceeded budgets refuse. Native storage supplies
compiler-checked existing static references and exact byte costs; it returns the
selected reference rather than manufacturing a lifetime. The serialized plan
retains source-qualified type and occurrence identities and revalidates against
a separate registry. Independent lookup cases and native pointer/lifetime controls
are distinct from complete native DTO reconstruction, original pointer provenance,
JSON number origins and physical CST ownership, which remain required. Local
source checks do not establish native execution.

Everything is extensible with decorators: one decorator API for the importer,
grammar rules, merge decisions, concept mappings, the executor and recovery,
CST → AST, transformations, emitters and translation rules. Decorators compose
in a defined order and are stored as links data that both packages share.
They also make translated output match the existing hand-written source.

The existing `importer` and `grammar-rule` hooks expose an opt-in `composition`
string. A nonempty value is one closed `any-grammar` or `all-grammar` Links
expression with `empty` as its insertion slot. Materialization replaces that
slot with the original owned rule expression, preserving the complete physical
grammar and its metadata. This bounded authoring form admits final normal rules
and nonempty, uniquely named candidates using one common `field-ranges` or
`json-string` profile; field candidates also require the same complete selector.
Malformed or unsupported data refuse rather than silently leave composition
unapplied. The empty field keeps the existing grammar path unchanged.

Decorators retain their existing order by `order` and then identifier. Applying
the same composition again retains an outer selection only when its mode and
complete validated candidate data, including candidate order and selectors,
agree. A different selection remains the original item of the new wrapper.
Removing or replacing a policy starts from the original grammar. Each source
grammar receives its own source-qualified policy before resolver or global
archive construction; a root option does not implicitly decorate its resolved
children or equate same-spelled rule names with source identities.

Composition uses the existing selection executor, mapped-tree checker and
shared parse budgets. Each source retains its complete physical CST; recursive
execution retains the derived CSTs and their shared ordered `sourcePlanes`
table. Text and binary archives must preserve both trees and derivation
witnesses. Authoring refuses composition with executor or recovery decorator
levels. Before publishing a tree with retained mapped source planes, the runtime
also refuses effective levels because their changes do not yet establish valid
mapped witnesses. This includes fallback success retaining attempted mappings,
materialized archives, resolved child grammars and parse-time overrides;
identity-only results retain their existing behavior. Native compilation and
runtime parity require CI verification.
This opt-in hook does not establish complete Markdown or other priority-language
coverage, and does not resolve scanner or imported semantic obligations.

The source-qualified composition batch draft binds ordered requests to exact
existing normal rules across one complete archived grammar inventory. A shared
sorted directory validates source/rule ownership, collisions and explicit text
and count bounds without a host/child language-pair table. The existing decorator
engine alone materializes each validated any/all template. The batch retains
four separate Links entries: the complete original discovery graph, the complete
composed discovery graph, ordered source-qualified requests and the actual typed
binding-decision definition. Reload eagerly reconstructs both inventories,
rederives compositions from the original context and compares the full current
graph, registrations, allocator history and selected decision definition.
Unselected grammars remain in the current common concept space. Original proof
contexts remain in their complete predecessor entry; changed definitions obtain
no correspondence merely from matching names. Existing source-view profiles and
parse budgets remain exact. The finite source controls return to an earlier
grammar through three source planes, with both text and binary reconstruction.
They are unexecuted drafts over explicit control grammars and establish neither
full catalog grammars nor new scanner, inference or equivalence capabilities.

The copying-sort draft adds dense immutable primitive-string `toSorted()` with
no comparator or extra arguments. It preserves the input, creates an independent
ordered result, and uses exact UTF-16 lexical order including isolated surrogates.
Both checkers and editable Links reload enforce the same operand/result domain;
JavaScript and Rust emitters use their stable standard sorting operations with
explicit unit comparison. This operation also supplies the composition batch's
shared sorted directories. The model follows
[ECMAScript copying sort](https://tc39.es/ecma262/multipage/indexed-collections.html#sec-array.prototype.tosorted)
and [Rust stable comparison sorting](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/primitive.slice.html#method.sort_by).
Independent primitive-source, complete graph/codec and native controls remain
unexecuted. General mutating sort, comparator effects, sparse/shared arrays,
non-string coercions and Lean/Rocq UTF-16 ordering remain required work.

Grammar work runs in bulk through the automated pipeline: import, merging and
transformation of every catalog language in one batch, adjustments recorded as
data-driven rules, never hand edits of generated grammars. Tree-sitter is only
a test oracle. Changes are drafted and debugged as a batch and pushed once;
nobody waits idle on CI.

The pinned Groovy grammar runs through this same native pipeline, with its
original parser confined to independent test oracles. Reviewed source rules
reconcile literal end sentinels with the actual input boundary, prefer complete
expressions at a context boundary, and require an argument value before
preferring an optional call attachment. These decisions retain the original
productions and generate shared executable Links Notation for both runtimes.
Context guards and preferred copies retain the source lexer token identities;
they do not create duplicate source token occurrences. Field-local ordering,
contextual keyword spellings and newline runs keep their decisions in grammar
data, with input-boundary lookahead that skips extras and emits no CST leaf.
The C++ and SQL imports use remembered-content and remembered-literal scanner
families. Grammar-owned raw-string punctuation surrounds an exactly matched
opening label; a whole dollar-quoted literal composes opening, content and
closing rules. SQL function bodies retain their separate surrounding grammar
and prevent a closing function label from starting an inner literal. Every
catalog SQL dialect has its own default-source expectation for the shared
grammar. Named choice aliases retain a complete boundary and the selected
production beneath it. Contextual clause exclusions preserve quoted identifiers
and longer words. A named NULL marker keeps absent-literal recovery visible.
Pattern-guarded context preferences retain their original fallback choices.
C++ scoped calls and assignments, dependent type segments and array allocation
dimensions are reconciled through these grammar rules and field preferences.
SQL pattern keywords keep named token identities while excluding identifier
continuations; a complete IS NOT operator retains its own concrete kind.
Dart, Nix and CMake scanner policies are generated through the shared fragment,
lexical token, nested delimiter and counted delimiter families. Fragments retain
paired-prefix parity, reject configured invalid boundaries and can require a
marker. Delimiter opening lookahead distinguishes documentation comments without
consuming a closing star. Lexical external rules retain following context, and
counted content records explicit whitespace and end policies. Scanner-only token
renames retain their original concrete kinds. A source variable or bracket opening
commits to its complete production before overlapping literal text can fall back;
case-insensitive command keywords retain word continuation boundaries. Adjacent
Nix path fragments continue one path, while whitespace separates applications.
These policies are grammar data executed identically by both runtimes. Dart and
Nix parser crates and the separate vendored CMake oracle crate are development
dependencies. The published runtime uses the generated native grammar text.
Required concept distinctions are shared data in
`parity/required-concept-distinctions.lino`; the record generator emits the
JavaScript JSON and Rust constant data from the same pairs and reasons. SQL
unknown values and Pascal null pointers keep separate concept identities.
Remembered textual delimiters use grammar actions to store opening tags and
predicates to compare closing tags exactly. The generated content scanner
preserves other tags inside the body and supports successive distinct tags
through the same shared state model.

## Continuous integration

Native feedback from run `39372455987` distinguishes actual compiler diagnostics,
fixture transport failures and translation defects. Current draft repairs retain
the original numeric algorithms, all 3,660 decimal and 3,628 projection controls,
and complete authenticated predecessor modules and fixtures as Links histories.
Native fixture transport retains exact UTF-16 units, including lone surrogates.
Generated module relocation changes lexical paths only; target-source string
literals and comments retain their original bytes. Shared optional-array result
decisions retain the receiver's actual element term in native inference.
Recorded finite source views and ownership styles produce the native integer
libraries automatically; their complete original JavaScript source stays exact.
These source checks and JavaScript observations do not establish native execution.

Frozen acquisition provenance and the active acquisition tool have separate
authenticated identities. The unchanged frozen asset inventory supplies the
distribution and decoded-input hashes; changing the downloader cannot replace
either check. Version-sensitive relative-character self-translation expectations
select the active package patch range and exact implementation source. They retain
every original item and full source round trip. Carried top-level effects remain
visible and complete translation remains an unmet obligation.
The raw native consumer's ten-second execution bound remains unchanged after
this run exceeded it while making progress. A recorded finite source lowering
now borrows 40 immutable primitive column locals across six readers. It retains
all complete source owners, interfaces and original invocation/result controls;
unknown source profiles refuse. Native execution and timing still require CI.
The corrected library packaging has an independently authenticated source-history
edge. It restores both complete prior receipts before their original gates,
while checking the actual corrected module and retaining every emitted algorithm,
archive and declaration. Historical source adapters likewise recheck complete
current bytes before restoring their original assertions. These views establish
source conservation and never supply native execution evidence.

Historical translation readers select an explicit source profile before older receipt inverses. One compact Links cohort authenticates complete committed raw descriptor epochs while reusing their existing certified archives. Array history recovers its original complete artifact; library relocation and continuation history retain the separately authenticated native packaging edge. Shared scalar facts preserve every logical alias. The first scoped-iteration declaration packet and the additional loop source each retain complete source memberships and byte inverses. Current parser and emitter files remain active. Every original receipt, producer assertion and strict whole-byte gate remains required; unknown readers or changed source bytes stay visible to those gates. These source views establish provenance conservation and supply no native execution or semantic-equivalence evidence.

CI follows the same order as development. One workflow
([`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml)) runs the
JavaScript workflow first and the Rust workflow after it: no Rust job starts
before every JavaScript job passed, and the Rust jobs show as skipped when
JavaScript fails. Inside the acceptance workflow, the Rust evidence stages,
the Rust native translations and the Rust consumers likewise need the
JavaScript stages.
Verification concurrency uses stable pull request numbers for updates and
individual run identifiers for other events, through every nested job group.
Release write jobs retain their separate non-cancelling queue. The retained
[checkpoint cancellation responses](../parity/fixtures/ci-concurrency-cancellation-2026-10-19.lino)
show an automatic collision on the former main-reference job group. The
annotation does not name the higher-priority run or a human cancelling actor.
Windows grammar deadlines select the logical suite even when the physical
matrix cell is named `physical`. A fresh-merge native phase retains its active
build-output lease across tests, doctests and examples, then cleans once the
whole phase exits. Every original command, assertion and deadline remains.
Registered native experiment inputs and shared Links data also activate the
Cargo lockfile, check and test prerequisite chain on pull requests. This
selection derives from the same native path filter and passes an explicit
Boolean to the called workflow. It preserves ordinary experiment and
documentation exclusions, the changelog policy and every earlier activation
condition; an unknown comparison base selects checks conservatively. A
JavaScript failure still prevents the Rust workflow from starting.

JavaScript evidence runs in separate group and shard jobs. Each physical
partition records its exact commit, selected test files, observations and logs
under a distinct identity. The aggregate requires every declared partition and
the complete test-file union before accepting the logical JavaScript suite;
missing, duplicate, mixed or foreign scope remains a gate error. The current
15 partitions cover all file groups within the existing 60-minute job limit.
Fourteen partitions run beside downstream workload producers; the downstream
partition retains its report dependencies. Both JavaScript collections must
succeed before Rust evidence starts, and both remain dependencies of the
aggregate with their original physical scope and artifact identities.
The Rust evidence suite starts only after the entire JavaScript matrix passes.
Its default-feature Cargo command retains every test target, the lockfile and
`--no-fail-fast`. It reuses the fresh-merge command's package-specific test
optimization with debug assertions and overflow checks explicitly enabled;
no release profile, feature selection or test filter replaces the suite.
Both suites provision their required formatter and compiler tools. Failure
tails and periodic command progress are retained in live CI output. Each
thirty-second heartbeat includes a bounded, credential-redacted actual log
suffix, so a job timeout retains recent test progress even if artifact upload
cannot run. Original command logs remain unchanged.
The Rust suite also forwards bounded, credential-redacted live output with
`--nocapture`, retaining the original command result and complete raw logs.
Scaling checks report their language, attempt, size and sample outside the
timed operation. These diagnostics preserve every workload, assertion, budget
and deadline; they do not establish the cause or resolution of a timeout.

The Formal AI workload may reuse CI build products in the explicitly selected
`RUNNER_TEMP/issue-195-formal-ai-build/target` directory. Its cache key binds the
compiler, platform, build profile, workload inputs and exact candidate crate;
a narrower key may reuse dependency outputs. Every run still creates fresh
consumers, verifies the candidate checksum and installed crate path, and runs
every workload and probe. Consumers, reports and probe outputs never enter this
cache. Build products are saved before task-owned cleanup; inherited Cargo target
variables do not expand that cleanup's scope. Warm-run timings remain CI evidence.

One logical Formal AI run uses two execution runners and a source-only canonical
merger. The corpus ratchet and remaining native workloads form disjoint exact
assignments from actual target-qualified `--list` inventories under all original
filters, including ignored tests. Both runners retain fresh consumers, run the
full JavaScript consumer before native work, execute both probes and preserve
the original resource limits. Both may restore the build cache above; only the
remaining-workload runner saves it. Partial linked reports bind the same commit,
run and attempt under distinct producer artifacts. The merger checks actual listed
and executed identities, compiler/platform/profile and final dependency-lock
equality, and candidate/source/input/output/probe/census evidence before applying
the unchanged full-report validators. Repeated JavaScript and probe executions
count once in the original full report. Missing, duplicate, foreign, tampered or
failed parts provide no complete evidence. The canonical job, report, artifact
and downstream barriers retain their identities, with full diagnostics retained.

Native tests belong under `rust/tests/`. Private output-comparison tests use an
isolated fixture extracted automatically from the actual owned Rust concrete
syntax declarations. Its source receipt binds every selected declaration and
the complete original test bytes; freshness checks compare the generated model
and receipt with those inputs. Compile-time references keep the copied fields
and operations checked without relaxing native lints or exposing private
production interfaces. This source extraction preserves the original algorithm
and assertions; it does not establish semantic self-translation or native test
execution before CI runs.

Expression-dispatch frame extraction records byte-exact original match arms
and parent reconstruction in
[`arm-preservation.lino`](../experiments/native-expression-dispatch-extraction/data/arm-preservation.lino).
Subsequent diagnostic source edits have a separate
[`following-diagnostic-source-change.lino`](../experiments/native-expression-dispatch-extraction/data/following-diagnostic-source-change.lino)
receipt. Their provenance does not establish native stack resolution, which
remains unverified. Original const and lexer refusal fixtures retain their exact
inputs and boundaries. The owned-array reserved-prefix source likewise remains
an exact negative; separate legal identifiers exercise the same actual emitted
temporary-name collisions.

The five native logical suites retain their original filters and Cargo commands.
Their platform test command uses the same package-specific optimized test
profile as fresh-merge and evidence execution: optimization level three, with
debug assertions and overflow checks explicitly enabled. The exact native
targets, all-feature selection, failure aggregation, physical assignments and
original deadlines are retained in the historical record below. Actual unoptimized Windows and Ubuntu deadline
diagnostics and complete preceding sources are retained in
[`native-test-profile-source-history.lino`](../parity/naming/native-test-profile-source-history.lino).
Independent controls inspect the raw current workflow and actual shell argument
expansion; historical source inverses retain every preceding guard. Configuration
checks establish no native timing or test outcome before CI execution.
Grammar, translation and remaining tests retain their four-partition base on
every original platform; inference and generative suites each keep one. Windows
translation subdivides each base partition into two, using eight physical cells
after CI39087221416 exceeded its fifteen-minute deadline. The same run required
eight-cell successors for Windows remaining tests, Ubuntu grammar and translation
tests, and Ubuntu instrumented grammar and translation coverage. Fresh Merge similarly
subdivides its original eight partitions into sixteen after a twenty-minute job
timeout. Both expansions preserve the exact union of every old hash partition,
all original filters and deadlines. After CI39113660901's macOS grammar test
partition exceeded its fifteen-minute job deadline, that profile also uses
eight physical cells. Its exact timeout annotation and source identity are
retained in
[`native-grammar-deadline-2026-10-18.lino`](../parity/fixtures/native-grammar-deadline-2026-10-18.lino).
Its logical filters, fifteen-minute deadline and complete inventory union remain
the acceptance contract; the successor's native timing still requires CI.
The same run's first Windows translation partition also exhausted fifteen
minutes. Its sixteen-cell successor separates the observed Lean and seeded
generative workloads from reconciliation while preserving each old eight-cell
inventory as an exact two-cell union. Windows coverage keeps its existing
eight-cell assignment. The frozen
[`native-translation-deadline-2026-10-18.lino`](../parity/fixtures/native-translation-deadline-2026-10-18.lino)
records the automatic timeout; the unchanged deadline and native work still
require successful successor execution.
The first Ubuntu translation partition later passed all sixty-eight native tests
in 525 seconds, then hit the same deadline during its final supplemental oracle.
Its sixteen-cell successor keeps all nine supplemental oracles with their
original owner and commands; Ubuntu translation coverage retains eight cells.
The same frozen timeout receipt records this additional source-specific result.
Other platform and suite assignments retain
their original counts. Fresh Merge uses the same fetched base, head and merged tree,
retaining all doctests and example linking in its first partition. Each Cargo
test executable supplies its complete inventory before partitioning. Links
receipts bind original filters, selected test identities, actual outcomes and
complete output to the run, attempt and source tree. The final aggregate checks
every admitted physical cell and the complete target and test union. Existing
oracle, doctest and example steps retain their original single owners. These
partitions preserve deadlines and acceptance scope; synthetic scheduling checks
do not establish native platform behavior or deadline fit.

CI run 39309745847 subsequently exhausted fifteen-minute platform cells after
cold optimized builds on Windows, macOS and Ubuntu. Complete logs and original
workflow and guard sources are retained in
[`platform-test-deadline-source-history.lino`](../parity/naming/platform-test-deadline-source-history.lino).
The current platform test job therefore has a finite thirty-minute deadline for
every physical cell. This supersedes the historical fifteen-minute timing
expectations above; it changes no matrix assignment, logical filter, test,
compiler safety argument, prerequisite, failure aggregation or other job limit.
Exact source inverses retain predecessor assertions, while independent controls
inspect the complete raw current workflow. CI must establish actual deadline fit.

An actual Clippy 1.99.1 similar-name diagnostic in the complete source-type
validator is corrected by renaming its fresh JavaScript binding from `type` to
`sourceTypeIndex` and regenerating every native declaration. The original
complete source, compiler diagnostics, all 33 source shapes and 14 parser
projections remain in
[`source-type-binding-name-source-history.lino`](../parity/naming/source-type-binding-name-source-history.lino).
The diagnostic observation is bounded to the recorded compiler patch range;
source-byte conservation and independent executable controls do not certify
native compilation or general semantic equivalence.

CI keeps expected-panic display annotations separate from actual test identities
and validates the native Windows linker after shell setup. Grammar-tool
operation failures retain their source errors and causes instead of becoming
syntax rejections. These diagnostic checks do not establish native success or
resolve an unexplained process failure.
The native child lookup may retry only the observed `ETIMEDOUT`, `SIGTERM`,
null-status process result, with at most three identical ten-second attempts.
Every successful lookup still validates its first resolved absolute linker
path. Other failures and exhausted retries remain fatal, and retry diagnostics
remain visible. Both Windows workflow owners share this exact script. Complete
preceding sources and the actual failed preflight are retained in
[`windows-linker-timeout-source-history.lino`](../parity/naming/windows-linker-timeout-source-history.lino);
isolated script controls establish no successor Windows execution result.

Every existing major-24 CI setup selects Node 24.22.0 exactly, with its bundled
npm 11.19.1, as recorded in the [official release metadata](https://nodejs.org/dist/index.json).
This prevents different runner caches from selecting different patches within
one logical evidence suite. The existing Node-22 report profile remains separate.
Whole-source controls preserve every command, matrix cell and deadline; historical
guard inputs have an authenticated patch-selection inverse. The complete
JavaScript and native partition mergers retain their exact toolchain equality
checks. Actual mixed-version records and failed aggregate diagnostics remain in
[`ci-node-runtime-source-history.lino`](../parity/naming/ci-node-runtime-source-history.lino).
Configuration checks supply no successor execution or full acceptance evidence.
Dependency collection compares every supplied Node release coordinate: exact
patch selectors use full-version comparison, moving minor selectors retain
their minor comparison and moving majors retain their major comparison. The
minimum-engine coverage profile remains explicit. A targeted registry update
records its own observation date and source; it preserves the last complete
audit date and every unrelated registry fact. Replacing a selector updates its
actual dependency references and retains the full preceding inventory and
collector in
[`ci-node-dependency-source-history.lino`](../parity/naming/ci-node-dependency-source-history.lino).
This does not substitute a one-source update for the complete dependency audit.
Historical source inventories may use a generated placement over a complete
destination file. The shared Links rule retains the original inventory/source
namespace, exact destination bytes and hash, complete original constructor and
every preceding placement. The generator refuses changed destinations or unknown
constructor edits. Current source meanings retain their own qualified identities;
a historical text view does not prove whole-program meaning equivalence.
New local generators enter the normal dependency inventory without replacing
earlier entries. Historical inventory assertions reconstruct their exact earlier
projection only after independently checking every actual current declaration;
the complete original prefix retains its item order, sharing and audit facts,
authenticated against the immutable Git source. A separate
current assertion checks the full live inventory and unchanged collector.
Recorded whole-module corrections retain every original input and assertion.
Current inventory witnesses identify actual current declarations explicitly;
archived definitions keep separate identities and authenticated source copies.
Complete raw source planning and the three whole-emission contract suites have
distinct required JavaScript CI owners. The explicit separate-suite selection
requires JavaScript CI with native execution disabled; default and native
invocation retain all original imports. Original assertions, commands, resource
limits, ten-minute deadlines and aggregate prerequisites remain checked through
exact source and workflow inverses. Splitting allocations does not establish
successful full-workload timing until CI executes the changed schedule.
The raw contract suite further separates eleven schema and consumer contracts
from seven complete observation-plan contracts into two required ten-minute
JavaScript cells. Explicit CI profiles register each original callback exactly
once across those cells, without skipped tests; the schema cell does not build
the full plan. Default invocation retains the whole suite, including its separate
native contract. Complete original test-call bodies, order, commands and limits
remain authenticated against their certified predecessor. Unknown profiles,
missing or duplicate registrations and native admission in a JavaScript-only
profile refuse. This follows CI 39489000471, which completed all three source
archives and constructed its observation plan before the unchanged deadline.
Successful timing and full native execution still require corrected-commit CI.
The complete tooling test group likewise has four required physical cells after
its measured fifteen-minute cancellation. The existing modulo file partitioner
assigns every current test file exactly once; all commands, assertions, flags,
deadlines and aggregate prerequisites remain intact. Historical workflow checks
recover their exact original inputs through a separate checked matrix inverse.
An exact native checked-program mismatch keeps its original rejection and adds
a bounded first differing field path. Declaration-list archive failures retain
their actual original input in the failure message. Neither diagnostic accepts,
normalizes or drops differing metadata or assertions. Hashed authored ANTLR
fixture and scanner-patch checkout bytes use LF on every platform.

Checked reference reconstruction distinguishes structural value payloads from
their allocations. A source-selected program projects nonreference arrays,
records, maps and sets through an internal closed payload constructor. Checking
still validates every operand, type, binding and source span; it suppresses only
the payload's own automatic allocation. An allocation unwraps only that immediate
payload boundary, retaining its original position around generated field-binding
chains. Nested operands retain ordinary allocation. Shared JavaScript rules,
automatically translated to Rust, select these forms and reject scalar or already
referenced results. Unknown, inherited, hidden, symbol and accessor wrapper fields
are refused; native transport preserves presence before rejecting unsupported
metadata. Unselected programs retain their prior projection shapes. The exact
checked-program comparison and original declaration-list inputs remain intact.
Bounded JavaScript controls retain complete graphs, alias identity, ordered field
effects, source spans, binding identities and full emission through both archive
formats. Native execution remains a separate CI check.

Original-source subprocess observations retain their existing execution,
compiler and output limits. Explicit compiler profiles are shared Links data:
the complete raw graph selects optimization with debug assertions and overflow
checks enabled, while the recorded text library selects short diagnostics.
Neither profile suppresses warnings. Bounded actual stdout and stderr remain
in separate diagnostic receipts and logs, including unsuccessful compiler
output; actual native processes also mirror those bytes to the CI log. The
original ordered producer bindings remain immutable. Additive source receipts
authenticate the old Git blobs and replay only reviewed runner changes, with
separate complete helper and verifier hashes. These transport and source checks
cannot supply successful native observations or a performance result.

Package validation runs nine required logical check groups across ten parallel
jobs: native imports, native fixtures, grammar, contracts, generated code and
source models, complete original-link tables, binary candidates and raw graph
preflight. The
generated group has two physical slices. The first retains the literal-edit
rule, fixture and experiment triplet, owned local container checks, strict public
declaration consumers and the reviewed generated-stage migration refresh. The
second retains every remaining generated check, including both compiler-feedback
producers and their single upload. All checks execute exactly once in their
original relative order;
each runner keeps the same setup and ten-minute deadline. Distinct concurrency
identities prevent sibling cancellation. All six earlier check-context names
remain, and the additional generated slice has its own name. The
source-model group executes the new raw-binding and complete text-certificate
checks, isolated owned-certificate adapters, original-identity and role
projection checks, complete String-symbol and eleven-table projection checks,
fresh collector certificates and library packaging generators
without adding work to the original generated-code group. Two additional matrix
cells own the complete seventeen-table projection and binary metadata candidate
checks. They retain the same setup, cleanup and ten-minute deadline. Their
native runner contracts execute with actual compilation disabled; separate
required Rust steps compile and execute the complete artifacts only after the
JavaScript workflow passes. The new cells preserve the original six matrix
groups, seven physical cells and check-context names. The final package job requires
every group, every JavaScript test partition and the complete report assertion
job before packaging or publication. It also requires the validated merge of
translation compiler feedback. Frozen step-conservation checks retain every
original command, condition and artifact owner exactly once across the groups.
The raw graph cell separately checks the complete automatic native generation,
the serial JavaScript graph contracts and the CI-only injected native oracle.
Its checks use a one-GiB heap bound and retain the existing ten-minute deadline.
Its actual native oracle is a separate parallel Rust job with the complete
existing Cargo test prerequisites and admission condition. It preserves the
fifteen-minute Ubuntu test deadline, requires a SHA-qualified observation
artifact and adds no work to the nine existing native oracles. Removing
only this cell, its three steps and the new job restores the previous workflows
byte for byte; the global JavaScript-first caller and path selector are unchanged.

The JavaScript package checks also compile the generated shared frontend rules
as a standalone Rust module with the crate's Clippy lint groups and warnings
denied. This checks the generated interface without building the crate's
dependencies. Compiler style fixes remain decorator data applied to a fixed
point by the generator.
The existing global-concept compiler check includes both closed generated
modules, the original correspondence verifier and its identity decisions, with
the same lint flags and original proof input. Actual raw diagnostics and both
source snapshots remain CI artifacts. Both style commands refuse native
compiler execution outside GitHub CI before creating compiler scratch. Explicit
`--feedback` replay remains available locally without native compilation.

Every job reports all of its failures in one run, not only the first: `cargo
test` runs with `--no-fail-fast`, every check step after the first runs unless
the run was cancelled, and `cargo fmt`, clippy and `cargo doc` are independent
steps. A repository test checks this structure.

The self-translation report jobs publish generic and decorated measurements
for every JavaScript source module, with the commit and hashes of both runtime
sources. Both acceptance suites verify the complete published reports before
recording the per-module difference assertion; a small local report test alone
cannot supply that evidence.
Each measurement may reuse extracted Rust function definitions for at most three
exact parser input strings across its generic, decorated and native comparisons.
Every comparison still runs. The row retains only function names and source
text, clears them on every exit and never caches a failed read or a CST. Distinct
source bytes require independent parsing. Full reports retain every module,
counterpart, coverage assertion and their existing CI deadline; timing success
requires a new CI observation.
Report import resolution checks every candidate callable and avoids resolving
dependencies of classes and forwarded names that the current source binder
cannot bind. Every source module still receives its complete measurement and
byte-coverage check.
The same report passes retain exact carried-item diagnostics and source ranges.
Grouping ranks distinct affected modules, retains the complete denominator and
exposes missing diagnostics in historical reports. Selected replay uses the
existing workers with pinned sources; it does not add a translation pass or
establish successful execution.
Import and export candidate discovery uses the shared lexer, retaining opaque
literals and nested scopes. Unsupported lexical identifiers or syntax use the
original grammar scan. Every candidate signature still requires checked
translation; this optimization cannot supply an unchecked signature.
In CI one JavaScript worker measures a module while the coordinator resolves
the following module's imports. Contexts and results travel as LiNo records;
completed rows retain their original order. Local reports remain sequential,
and CI keeps the same report deadlines and verification gates.

The initial translation, native grammar and conformance test groups run in disjoint CI file
partitions. Their union retains every original file exactly once, and each job
keeps the existing time limit. File partitioning does not change assertions,
fixtures, runtime obligations or the requirement scope.
The initial native grammar group uses six file partitions after
[CI39125289901's captured deadline](../parity/fixtures/javascript-native-grammar-deadline-2026-10-18.lino).
Each original three-way partition is exactly the union of two current
partitions. The JavaScript job deadline remains fifteen minutes.
Six initial translation partitions retain the fifteen-minute deadline after
run 39394008799 exhausted it while still progressing. Each
transports feedback with a LiNo receipt of the exact commit, selected files and
payload hashes. A required merge validates every partition, permits an empty
individual partition, rejects missing, stale or conflicting inputs, and restores
the original combined artifact name, paths and retention. An empty complete
collection still fails. Acceptance keeps its separate evidence records, with
six translation partitions and twenty total physical scopes. Every current test
file must occur exactly once in the complete scope union before the logical
suite can supply evidence. Missing, mixed, stale, failed or altered partitions
refuse. Original sixty-minute evidence deadlines, compiler checks, commands,
artifact paths, toolchain pins and all other workflow bytes remain unchanged.
Historical guard views retain the certified original fifteen-scope workflow;
independent current controls check the complete six-partition workflow inverse.
Capture and merge explicitly select six partitions; the transport's historical
three-part API remains separately checked. Complete original workflow bytes stay
authenticated for historical controls, and independent controls inspect the
actual current six-way matrix, feedback cohort and native artifact uploads.

A separate JavaScript test group checks these published reports after all
report shards finish. It uses the producers' exact checkout and source hashes,
reuses their measurements, and must pass before Rust or delivery stages start.
Standalone JavaScript workflow runs produce and check their own report shards.

Real target-tool interchange reports identify the emitted target format, its
tool and version, the accepted and rejected corpus inputs and any failed cycle.
A Peggy report verifies the Peggy target only; it cannot satisfy the obligation
to execute cycles under every other real grammar tool. Development oracle
corrections build in CI and keep their original source hashes and a separately
identified correction, so the native runtime remains independent of the oracle.

Module scope may additionally admit a constant initializer whose complete CST
contains only Number literals, arithmetic operators and parentheses. Its actual
lexical name still participates in standard-owner shadowing. Calls, references,
BigInt, coercion from other values, assignments and eager sibling effects refuse.
Shared JavaScript decisions generate the native classifier and both checked
Links archives. Classification neither folds nor translates the initializer and
never supplies a callable signature for a carried binding. Both host adapters
retain the actual CST binder when the portable lexer lacks its Unicode spelling;
this ownership transport does not establish general Unicode translation. Strict
module arithmetic excludes legacy leading-zero spellings. This admits ordinary
size constants such as `128 * 1024 * 1024` while retaining separate translation
and native execution obligations.

An independently translated pure Number module initializer may supply its actual
checked scalar static signature to a sibling. Complete CST ownership, a matching
name, the existing unique-owner/module-scope proof and a successfully emitted
initializer are required. The ordinary emitter retains the original arithmetic
and reads its existing lazy static; no alternate evaluator or copied semantics
are introduced. Containers, calls, BigInt and unsupported operators do not gain
this interpretation. TypeScript ownership scans use the same real erasure path
as its ordinary frontend and native binder. Source restoration remains exact;
independent Number behavior and whole-result parity require execution in CI.

The borrowed JavaScript function-body host retains its preceding scoped-iteration
source through complete Git-certified Links data and seven exact borrowing edits.
Only the existing historical readers request that predecessor. Current source
bytes, unknown-edit refusals, original packets and both source codecs retain
independent checks; source conservation supplies no native execution evidence.

Compiler-host source succession also retains the complete preceding crate root,
Boolean hosts and ASCII native artifact as independently certified Links data.
Every checked program, interface, source definition and replay field of the ASCII
artifact stays exact; only its certified generated native files differ. Historical
Boolean author modes preserve current physical hosts. The active crate-root
receipt adds this source succession while keeping its whole earlier recipe and
all actual current reexports; native CST owner proofs inspect the complete current
root, including the newly public constructor. Older root receipts remain valid
for their original bytes, and unknown source edits still refuse.
Crate fixture packaging similarly retains both complete certified manifests and
the exact two-entry inverse. Historical tooling capture selects its original
manifest only through that proof; actual packaging checks keep the current one.
Operation registration preserves the published order when new declarations are
added to an existing source module, appending new identities after every old row.
The operation extractor reads the complete formal parameter list before locating
its body, so destructured/default parameters cannot masquerade as a definition.
Two earlier parameter fragments are corrected from the same complete original
Git-certified modules, registration receipt and codec profile. The proof retains
both original fragments, every identity and unchanged implementation/import
context; the exact field inverse preserves the old metadata scope. Unknown
source contexts or definition edits refuse. Declaration offsets in this profile
use UTF-16 code units, as the original JavaScript source lexer and string slices
do. Both Links codecs preserve complete original/current definition fields.
This metadata correction does not claim program equivalence between changed
implementations or complete recursive semantic definitions for every concept.

Historical naming storage rereads every complete document before selecting its
cached immutable text. Only equal scalar strings share storage; returned index,
document and decoded containers remain detached. One actual root and its five
registered paths bound the cache. Every whole-file hash, source certificate,
serializer comparison and final reread remains mandatory, including page edits
behind an unchanged index and distinct copied roots. Decoder and memory limits
remain unchanged.

## Resource limits

Source integration can stage every generated Rust module part directly from its
complete Links generation recipe with `js/scripts/stage-source-library-files.mjs`.
The helper bounds recipe size and file count, requires actual regular project
paths and exact recorded source bytes, and preserves unrelated staged changes.
Edited/stale outputs and symlink/path escapes refuse. This source-membership
review supplies no behavior, compilation or semantic-equivalence evidence.

Grammar test files run exactly once across six required JavaScript cells. Their
commands, assertions, fail-fast setting and fifteen-minute deadline remain exact.
The source history retains the complete preceding three-cell schedule and its
certified inverse. This change follows CI 39489000471, whose first grammar cell
passed all 335 tests before teardown reached GitHub's deadline. Corrected timing
requires the next CI run.

Offline dependency declaration refresh retains the previous ordered identities,
updates facts from current declarations and appends new identities. It does not
advance the registry audit date. The original 745-record prefix and the complete
773-record predecessor remain independently reconstructible from certified Links
sources; neither old facts nor current declaration checks are discarded to repair
an order failure.

Native coverage uses Cargo's optimized test profile with debug assertions and
overflow checks explicitly enabled. The complete instrumented commands, line
coverage floor, partitions and fifteen-minute deadline remain mandatory. Fresh
merge jobs may restore the successful native check dependency cache before
executing their complete commands; a cache miss still runs normally. An artifact
service finalization failure may trigger one upload retry of the identical
complete native partition. The final upload must succeed and missing payloads
remain errors. These execution policies do not establish successful native
observations until the corrected commit actually runs in CI. Cargo's documented
[profile controls](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/profiles.html)
define these compiler options.


Local sandboxes and CI runners have a few gigabytes of memory. The tooling
keeps every run within that bound instead of relying on a larger machine.

Optional source-catalog authoring can reuse an authored root coordinate after
every complete current asset digest and byte count matches its snapshot entry.
The actual asset name, qualified identifier and root shape must also match.
The incremental command is `npm run draft:source-definition-catalog:incremental`
in js. Both package twins are still read and compared, regular-file and source
size bounds remain, and changed graphs use the original streaming metadata
reader. Cached root coordinates establish neither graph validation nor concept
equivalence. The author cache reads Links snapshots up to four MiB and otherwise
uses the complete authoring path. Default --draft still reads every graph;
--check always ignores cache entries and retains the original whole-network
reader. No scoped/cached check mode is accepted. Complete preceding tool source
and independent cache/fallback/gate controls remain retained and deferred.

Primitive owner-path resolution is a separate shared source module from method
registration and arithmetic plans. Associated calls delegate to that exact
path domain; From and integer constants import it directly, with From retaining
its separate explicit binary64 extension. This keeps unrelated operation
implementations out of conversion/constant/expression/default source libraries.
The factoring draft retains seven complete original generation recipes before
emission. An obsolete generated native part is removed only when its path is
absent from the new recipe and its complete checkout bytes equal the recorded
original recipe and committed source. Its full old source remains reconstructible
from the retained recipe; unknown edits refuse removal. Receipts record paths,
original hashes and byte counts without claiming behavioral equivalence.

Five JavaScript typed-definition graphs shrink from 12,145,773 to 3,766,359
bytes; the Rust twins carry the same source change. These are source artifact
sizes, not runtime performance measurements. 548 exhaustive path-domain controls
retain ordinary/shadow/absolute/qualification premises, all primitive owners,
malformed paths and From's separate f64 scope. 190 complete retained programs
cover all preceding integer-constant sources plus explicit Boolean/binary64
defaults. Both runtimes/codecs, four emissions, original/native products and
predecessor reconstruction remain deferred controls. This source factoring
establishes no concept-meaning identity or executed runtime evidence.

Live cleanup fixtures use the existing JavaScript runtime at their original
fixture and tool paths, with the same lifetime and retention/removal assertions.
Hard links avoid duplicating its binary; only a cross-device failure permits a
copy. The fixture never changes permissions on a hard link. This also avoids
depending on the dispatch behavior of a renamed system multicall utility.
Dependency inventories and all other manifest inputs must finish changing before
the final requirement-manifest refresh and freshness check.

For the current task, the [latest instruction](../parity/sources/solo-continuation-2026-10-21.txt)
requires existing agents to finish and the coordinator to continue alone without
spawning or reactivating agents. It follows the
[three-agent automation phase](../parity/sources/automation-first-own-codebase-baseline-2026-10-21.txt), the
completed [solo continuation phase](../parity/sources/solo-continuation-2026-10-19.txt)
and earlier [two-subagent allowance](../parity/sources/two-subagent-continuation-limit-2026-10-18.txt).
These are successive task phases, not a permanent project-wide cap. Keep source
ownership disjoint, serialize resource-heavy local checks and Git index writes,
and integrate shared metadata once per complete batch. One coordinator pushes
only after the preceding CI finishes and its errors have concrete repairs.
Native compilation and expensive workloads remain exclusive to CI.
CI 39937987715 exceeded the unchanged ten-minute package deadline while replaying
three complete Rust sources. Source preparation now proceeds serially while each
ready source starts its existing archive worker. All three text and binary CST
replays and successful worker exits still precede fixture/matrix allocation.
Every factory and worker settles before publication or aggregate failure. The
coordinator owns the factory inventory and reserves an extra GiB when selecting
capacity; the original CPU, explicit job-count and one-GiB worker bounds remain.
Tiny injected scheduling tests establish ordering and retention, not improved
whole-source timing. Corrected timing requires CI; full raw workloads stay there.
The subsequent raw job in CI 39951766736 still exceeded ten minutes after all
three complete archive replays and matrix construction. One invocation now
shares its actual checked program between the ordinary current and consuming
emission helpers, instead of repeating parse/check/current emission. Every
original source pin, 106-owner comparison, consuming codec replay and final
profile assertion remains. The temporary checked graph is released after
consuming preparation, including failure; it is never retained across plans.
This removes repeated preparation, without claiming corrected CI timing.
CI 39960718444 still exceeded that deadline just after plan construction.
Complete stored source candidates now start every available archive worker
before fresh compiler preparation begins. Candidates supply no compiler or
profile authority: their entire text must equal each freshly generated source,
and the original complete source, owner, profile and codec checks still run.
Every worker and fresh validation settles before aggregate failure or
publication; fixtures and the observation matrix wait for both phases. The
original three complete CST text/binary replays, CPU/heap bounds and ten-minute
deadline remain. Worker diagnostics record elapsed time at their source so
buffered output cannot conceal the phase durations. Whole-workload timing
remains a CI observation.
Historical naming selection preflights every selected current source's complete
bytes and digest before running its historical extractors. It keeps a private
per-invocation read map, checks every selected epoch's pin and discards the map
afterward. All original per-view reads, hash assertions, extractor calls and
current-name exclusions still execute after successful preflight. Unknown edits
therefore refuse before expensive unrelated extraction without acquiring any
historical authority. This addresses the tooling shard in CI 39960718444 that
passed all 173 tests but exceeded its existing fifteen-minute job deadline.
The corrected timing still requires CI.
That run also exposed an obsolete refusal for the retained `const LIMIT = 1 + 2`
fixture. The expected translation now reflects the actual source-owned Number
static support. Every old input and round-trip assertion remains; an additional
control requires JavaScript and generated Rust to execute the original source's
result of three. Active Node versions are bounded to `>=22.15 <23` or `>=24 <25`,
and native execution to Rust `>=1.90 <1.100`; uncovered versions refuse.
The serial final verification also runs the existing JavaScript crate-input
package check after all seventeen preceding checks. Generated native unit
fixtures live in the crate data directory with identical shared Links inputs
and explicit package entries; the check does not invoke Cargo.

Report modules retain eight required jobs; general grammar tests retain six.
Their complete current source inventories are scheduled using source-bound
elapsed-time samples captured automatically from the fourteen complete report
and grammar job logs of CI 39615628279. Missing or changed sources receive a
size estimate. All modules and test files still execute exactly once, and
native and historical modulo partitions keep their existing ownership rules.
Job counts, commands, coverage assertions and deadlines remain unchanged.
These samples and scheduling estimates establish no requirement observation;
only the next CI execution can confirm actual timing and results.

CI 39839936265 reached the existing fifteen-minute deadline in four translation
cells and one conformance cell. The current physical schedule splits each of
the six logical translation cohorts into two measured subsets and each of the
four logical conformance cohorts into two subsets. Each pair preserves its
complete original file inventory exactly once. All twelve translation feedback
partitions must retain their exact commit and file scope before merging; missing
or foreign partitions still fail. Execution remains serial within each runner,
and commands, assertions, native checks and the fifteen-minute deadline retain
their existing obligations. The complete earlier workflow and its finite inverse
remain authenticated separately. These scheduling controls establish no passing
test observations; CI must verify the actual workloads.

The twenty complete report, grammar and translation job logs of CI 39644810104 add a
second source-bound timing cohort. The newest matching complete source wins;
an older matching source remains available when the newer cohort has no sample.
Only the eight report cells, six general grammar cells and six translation
cells use these costs.
Other report geometries retain their original byte-balance contract. No required
job, complete module, assertion or deadline is removed or relaxed. GitHub's
thirty-minute report deadline remains mandatory, including checkout time.
The cancelled fifth report completed only part of its assigned source inventory;
its partial progress never supplies a complete report or passing observation.

CI 39848242758 published all eight complete report artifacts, but GitHub marked
the fifth job cancelled for exceeding its thirty-minute job deadline, including
checkout and teardown. The completed report steps do not override that failed
gate. Complete bounded logs from all eight jobs supply a new advisory cohort
for every one of their 345 source modules. Only the eight report cells select
these newest source-bound costs; older grammar and translation samples retain
their current selection. Missing or changed source uses the existing estimate.
Every current module still runs exactly once, with unchanged commands, complete
report assertions and the thirty-minute deadline. Further CI must confirm timing.

CI 39857274367 also produced all eight complete reports; its second job exceeded
the same deadline. Its 347 complete, source-bound samples form a further advisory
report cohort. Both earlier cohorts and their controls remain available. The
selected costs affect ownership only; they confer no completion or execution
authority, and all eight jobs, full module coverage and deadlines remain required.
The two report workers reuse whichever slot finishes first. A slow preceding
measurement does not leave the other worker idle. Context resolution and final
publication retain their original source order; failures and complete module
coverage remain mandatory. The worker count and thirty-minute deadline are
unchanged. A controlled slow-first regression checks this scheduling boundary;
complete report timing still requires CI.

CI 39872149597 passed seven report cells, while the second exceeded the same
thirty-minute limit before completing its module inventory. Each of the original
eight logical report cells now runs as two physical parts. The source-bound
scheduler partitions complete modules within each logical cell; their disjoint
union must equal that cell exactly. Both workflows publish distinct artifacts
for all sixteen physical parts. The existing aggregate still requires every
current source module exactly once, matching commit and source hashes, both
translation comparisons and every report assertion. No partial report counts as
success. The thirty-minute job deadline and two-worker limit remain unchanged;
the original eight-cell command remains supported. Actual timing requires CI.
CI 39878097119 passed all sixteen physical report jobs. Complete certified
workflow predecessors keep the older evidence-partition conservation control
on its exact original bytes. Both committed workflow versions and every report
edit are independently checked; unknown successor bytes receive no historical
view. Actual current report coverage still uses the sixteen-part controls.

Source-only package checks run alongside the JavaScript test matrix. They use
fresh checkouts and consume no test or report artifacts; the final JavaScript
aggregate still requires tests, reports, every package check and merged compiler
feedback. Rust and acceptance keep their complete JavaScript prerequisites.
The scheduling change retains both independently committed complete workflows
and an exact one-line forward/inverse proof. Unknown successors receive no
historical view, and all previous workflow conservation assertions remain.

Reviewed compiler feedback corrects generated library styles through the normal
Links decorators. Complete original Git-certified source files and the original
rendered diagnostic log remain separate from machine-applicable suggestions.
Corrections preserve lexical references, source strings and comments; Boolean
normalization retains operand order, NaN and signed zero. Independent Boolean
facts and complete ordered CST columns retain their shared interfaces through
specific lint expectations that must remain fulfilled. The strict compiler and
lint gates continue to run in CI. Formatting, source replay and JavaScript
controls do not establish native execution.

The raw-oracle runner may reuse a successfully verified base emission profile
within one process only after comparing every complete historical/current
artifact, rule source and causal producer input byte again. Input names and order
also match. Changed, omitted, reordered or added inputs invoke the original
complete verifier; failures and mutation during verification never enter the
cache. Returned values have independent ownership. Consuming/borrowed profile
checks and complete transport source authentication still run around each actual
or injected invocation. This avoids repeatedly decoding unchanged large Links
artifacts; it does not replace source replay, native execution or fixture checks.

Serial metadata authoring refreshes both packaged canonical concept catalogs
before final naming verification, and retains the separate evidence-translation
naming and source archives after CI source repairs. Catalog authority still
requires exact canonical data and actual tooling owners. Lower peak allocation
changes evaluation order only; every foundation rejection and source assertion
remains mandatory.
Canonical tooling counts exact UTF-8 input bytes without allocating complete
encoded copies. UTF-16 surrogate replacement and every existing physical and
expanded byte limit retain their original behavior. Boundary controls compare
the active UTF-8 encoder, including paired and unpaired surrogates.
The repository's serial development driver also provides a final `verification`
phase. Seventeen read-only checks reconcile live requirement sources before ledger
access, then check dependency/scope/export inventories, documentation, test
ownership, generated native rules, source histories and both concept catalogs.
Its separate registration phase invokes the normal append-only operation,
complete binding and codec-epoch authors in order before catalog generation.
A failed author retains its receipt and prevents dependent registration.
The final source-history check validates all indexed naming documents, their
source certificates, every current source pin and exact serialization.
Missing or stale data stops the phase without refreshing it. This consolidates
the existing bounded checks; full suites, reports and native execution remain
separate CI obligations. Invocation receipts are not requirement observations.

Complete workflow and scheduling test successors, and the crate manifest, are independently certified at
8f2f6c48, with their complete predecessors certified at 8bc0ef97. Historical
tooling and scheduling guards select only those exact byte inverses. Current
checkout provenance, current measured scheduling and their new controls execute
separately. Unknown source successors still refuse.

The normal supplemental naming author generates and verifies its complete
output in fresh serial processes. The author receipt binds every output byte;
the unchanged authenticated reader validates every source certificate and its
normal serialization must reproduce that digest before the command succeeds.
Equal immutable source strings may share storage; independent ordered rows,
certificates and containers retain their identities. This keeps the same checks
within the existing local heap budget without reducing inventories or gates.
The normal author derives supplemental views with the same inventory extractors
in a disposable JavaScript worker capped at 352 MiB. It waits for one result and
successful worker exit before encoding the full output. Every source epoch,
ordered inventory, current source pin, extracted name and record mapping remains
mandatory; the exported check path retains its complete in-process comparison.
The extraction heap expires before serialization so their largest graphs do not
overlap. The existing process-tree resource guard still bounds the entire task.

Supplemental historical naming sources use an explicit Links index once their
combined data outgrows one closed graph. Four independently closed documents
retain the complete ordered source epochs at the registered revisions. The index
records every path, revision, byte count, digest and source/view count. Every
document's complete bytes are checked before cache reuse; every decoded source
still passes its independent Git certificate. Legacy single-graph input retains
its original decoder and limits. Each indexed document uses those same limits;
the combined history is not presented as one graph that passed the old budget.
Normal verification reproduces every page and index byte, preserves source/view
order and refuses a container alias that would cross documents. Cache results
retain independent containers while sharing only immutable scalar storage.

The canonical bootstrap reader may share equal immutable metadata inside its
private graph, which never escapes. It still allocates every original data link,
distinguishes independent containers, preserves actual aliases, and charges every
original stored/expanded node and byte. Language, shape, reachability, cycle and
other validation rules remain unchanged. This reduces peak allocation without
raising the task or codec limits.

Repeated historical naming reads reuse one authenticated immutable snapshot.
Every packet read checks its complete serialized input before lookup;
snapshot reuse requires equal complete serialized text, and changed
text runs every source-certificate check again. Callers receive detached copies,
and selecting a historical name still checks the complete actual source in the
requested root. Raw descriptor selection privately reuses the already checked
current descriptor packet. These caches retain no failure and change no source
scope, assertion, output or resource ceiling.

Historical inventory generation finishes Git object capture before loading the
complete naming inventory. Git pack buffers and the decoded binding graph use
separate memory lifetimes. Full certificate, extraction, freshness and byte
inverse checks remain required under the same local resource limits.

Self-translation reuses each source group's existing declaration scan when
classifying primitive literal bindings. A proven named function cannot satisfy
the literal classifier's zero-declaration condition; its ordinary complete parse,
check and target emission still run. Dependency-history selection first rejects
invalid current declaration inventories with the existing complete inventory
check. Successful inputs still authenticate every original certificate and
historical byte. These changes remove redundant work without extending budgets
or changing the independent CI report and acceptance gates.

Canonical Links Notation writing combines bounded groups of complete statements
before assembling the final text. This bounds temporary string construction while
retaining every statement, UTF-16 code unit, source order, metadata field,
registration decision, line ending and host observation.

Each bulk grammar worker reads projection metadata from its selected actual
generated grammar. The fixture generator uses the same reader, while unrelated
grammar projections and corpus definitions stay outside that worker's setup.
Inventory fields, source names, missing-projection refusals and independent
oracle comparisons retain their existing meaning. Bounded selected-grammar
controls check fidelity; the complete workload and its timing remain CI checks.
CI progress separates worker execution, report preparation, global construction
and both archive serializations using actual elapsed times.

Use one shared repository checkout for ordinary development, review and bounded
JavaScript checks, with explicit ownership of edited paths. Do not create a
worktree or clone for each agent, test or review. The coordinator may select an
isolated task worktree when the task requires it; share the existing dependency
installation rather than duplicating it. After its commits are preserved on a
named branch or integrated and no process or active task uses it, release that
task-owned worktree promptly with `git worktree remove` without `--force`.
Before removal, verify tracked and untracked changes and inspect ignored
contents for retained evidence or unique work. Preserve dirty worktrees,
evidence, active tasks and unrelated directories. A cache-cleanup run does not
release sibling worktrees. Cleanup is restricted to meta-language-owned storage;
it never clears unrelated project or user data. Rust compilation runs only in
CI/CD. Local Rust checks are limited to formatting changed files.

For local cleanup and wrapped checks, unset inherited `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and
`CARGO_BUILD_TARGET_DIR` unless their resolved directories have been verified
as storage owned by the current meta-language task. The cleanup tool also reads
those environment variables; a valid Cargo cache marker establishes the kind
of directory, not which project owns its contents. Keep manual local cleanup
within the selected project scope, disable automatic low-disk escalation and
container cleanup with `--no-preflight --no-docker`, and retain the cleanup
receipt. Deliberately selected external caches still require that ownership
check before use.
Task-scoped cleanup and wrapped local checks also pass `--project-owned-only`.
This policy excludes explicit and inherited Cargo target paths outside the
selected project before discovery or byte accounting. Its filesystem scope
still admits the project's ignored caches and stopped temporary scratch with
an exact matching ownership marker, while retaining the existing source,
evidence, active-task and other-worktree checks. It disables container cleanup
and automatic low-disk escalation and records `project-owned-only` in the receipt.

Linux cleanup walks process ancestry entirely in the PID namespace of the
mounted procfs. It excludes ancestors before translating known process IDs to
the caller's namespace. This preserves running test and Cargo processes when
an outer procfs is visible and numeric IDs collide between namespaces; foreign
